Audited every mode-change reducer case (`set-agent-mode`, `set-workspace-mode`) in `create-session-draft-model.ts` against "reset only what the new mode invalidates": `set-agent-mode` (single/multi) resets nothing, only conditionally seeds empty seats, correctly preserving `agentId` across a round trip since it is not invalidated by seat mode, just temporarily unused. `set-workspace-mode` resets `worktreeEnabled` unconditionally on every toggle, which looks like an over-reset on a remote→local return — traced it through and it is not observable: `worktreeEnabled` can only become `true` while `LocalWorkspaceSection` is mounted (i.e., already in local mode), so it is always already `false` by the time a mode round trip could matter; no path exists where a valid `true` value is lost. New `tests/e2e/create-session-wizard.spec.ts` (2 tests, real browser, not reducer-only) proves both properties end-to-end rather than by inspection alone: a full Step-4→Step-1→Step-4 round trip preserves a typed project path and session title verbatim, and a local→remote→local workspace-mode round trip resets the worktree checkbox to unchecked while the project path typed before the round trip survives untouched. Both passed.
- [ ] 11.9 Add recent-session templates or recent creation presets only when their data is non-sensitive and validated. — Not built; deferred rather than guessed at. No `design.md` entry names this feature's shape (grepped for "recent-session template"/"recent... preset" — no hits), and the task's own "only when their data is non-sensitive and validated" qualifier reads as an intentional off-ramp for exactly this situation: a template spanning Agent + workspace + personalization would need to deliberately exclude remote-workspace fields (`remoteHost`/`remoteUser`/SSH connection id — credential-adjacent) from whatever it captures, which is a real design decision (what counts as a safe subset, how it is presented, whether it is per-workspace or global) with no existing precedent to reuse. Distinct from and not satisfied by two adjacent, narrower mechanisms already in place: `knownProjects`' recent-project quick-select (`create-session-workspace-sections.tsx`, workspace-path recall only) and `previousSessionAgentStorageKey` (`create-session-agents.ts`'s `defaultSessionAgent`, which — read closely — only special-cases restoring OnePiece specifically, not a general "remember the last Agent" mechanism). Left for a follow-up with real design input rather than a guessed-at template shape.
- [x] 11.10 Place validation and discovery errors at the owning field or step and provide a Review-level error summary with links. — "Discovery errors" (a failed path inspection, a failed submission) were already surfaced pre-wizard via `lifecycle.error` in the dialog's persistent footer banner, visible across every step; unchanged. "Validation errors" (task 11.1's per-field `CreateSessionValidationReason` codes) had zero display anywhere — `canAdvancePastStep` already blocked Next/Create on them, silently, with no explanation. Added: 9 reason codes translated across all 5 locales (`createSession.validation.*`); Step 2 shows the active `agent`/`seats` reason (mutually exclusive — only one of the two sections is ever mounted) below the Agent/seat picker; Step 3 shows the active `workspace` reason below the local/remote form (`sshConnection` deliberately excluded — `RemoteWorkspaceSection` already shows it inline at the field it describes, predating this task); Step 4 gained a Review-level summary block, shown only when `agent`/`seats`/`workspace` is still non-null, each entry a "Fix" link calling the wizard's existing `goToStep`. Traced when that summary can actually appear given the strict step gates: not through ordinary forward navigation (an invalid step 2/3 already blocks Next before step 4 is reachable), but a live external change (an Agent losing availability, a background refetch) while already sitting on step 4 can invalidate `validation.agent`/`seats` without any navigation at all — a narrow but real case, not a hypothetical one, and exactly what a static "Create is disabled" button with no explanation would otherwise leave silent. Verified at both layers: `create-session-step-4.test.tsx` (4 new tests) exercises the Review summary directly with constructed validation states, since normal forward-only wizard navigation cannot reach step 4 in an invalid state to trigger it through the UI; `create-session-wizard.spec.ts` gained 2 new real-browser tests proving the Step 2/Step 3 owning-field messages for real (workspace-path-missing visible on arrival at Step 3 and clearing once filled; seats-too-few appearing after removing a seat below the 2-seat minimum). Also removed two orphaned i18n keys (`createSession.review.override`/`titleOverridden`) found dead while working in this exact area — leftover from task 11.7's design pivot away from a passive "override" summary row to the real editable title field, never cleaned up.
- [x] 11.11 Prevent duplicate submission and destructive dismissal while creation commits without freezing scroll, copy, or help. — Most of this was already true, inherited from the shared `ApplicationDialog`/`useFocusTrap` primitives predating this wizard, not built here: `closeDisabled={lifecycle.loading}` already blocks the backdrop-click handler (`application-dialog.tsx`) *and* the Escape key (`use-focus-trap.ts:36`) while a submission is in flight, and the footer's Back/Cancel button already carries `disabled={lifecycle.loading}` too -- verified by reading all three, not assumed. Neither primitive imposes `overflow: hidden`/`user-select: none`/anything else while closing is disabled, so scrolling and copying inside the dialog body stay live; there is no help affordance in this dialog to freeze. The one real gap: `submit()` (`use-create-session-draft.ts`) called `submitCreateSession` unconditionally, relying solely on the Create button's `disabled` state (a render away from the click) to prevent a second invocation -- added a synchronous `if (lifecycle.loading) return;` guard at the top of the closure, which cannot be beaten by timing the way a state-driven disabled attribute technically can. Verified end-to-end in `create-session-wizard.spec.ts`: clicks Create, then attempts Escape and a backdrop click while the operation is still polling (`use-create-session-draft.ts`'s real 600ms `operationService` poll gives a real, non-instant window to test against), asserts the dialog survives as exactly one instance and Back stays disabled, then lets the real operation finish so the test does not leave a dangling poll.
- [x] 11.12 Use a standard dialog at wide width and full-height sheet at compact width with virtual-keyboard-safe footer. — `create-session-dialog-content.tsx` now picks `ApplicationDialog` (centered, `max-w-2xl`) at `min-width: 640px` and above, `Sheet` `placement="full"` below it, via the codebase's existing `useMediaQuery` hook (`hooks/use-media-query.ts`, already used the same way by `work-board.tsx`/`skills-page.tsx`/`prompt-hook-card-list.tsx`, each with their own content-appropriate breakpoint -- 640px matches `ApplicationDialog`'s own existing `p-3 sm:p-5` breakpoint rather than inventing a new one). `footer`/`title`/`description`/`onClose`/`closeDisabled` are built once and passed to whichever primitive renders, since both share that exact prop shape. `Sheet` was already built for this (design.md Decision 3, `Sheet.tsx`'s own doc comment) but nothing in the codebase used `placement="full"` for a *modal* before this. "Virtual-keyboard-safe footer" is where the two primitives structurally differ in a way that matters here: `ApplicationDialog` bounds itself with an explicit `max-h-[calc(100vh-1.5rem)]`, and `100vh` is the well-known mobile pitfall (the *layout* viewport, which some mobile browsers do not shrink for an on-screen keyboard, so a footer near the bottom can end up hidden behind it) -- `Sheet`'s `placement="full"` instead sizes via `inset-0` on a `position: fixed` ancestor, which tracks the *visual* viewport, the correct behavior. This was not built newly for this task; routing narrow-width usage to `Sheet` inherits it for free. Not independently provable in Playwright (no real on-screen keyboard to trigger), stated honestly rather than claimed as verified. What *is* verified: `create-session-wizard.spec.ts`'s 2 new tests confirm the actual breakpoint switch fires (bounded, off-center box at 1440px; edge-to-edge box exactly matching viewport width at 500px).
- [x] 11.13 Preserve current service input semantics and add contract fields only where required by already supported modes. — Verified rather than assumed: `CreateSessionInput` (`src/types/agent.ts:524`) was never edited anywhere in this task group's work, confirmed by re-reading its current definition against `submitCreateSession`'s construction of it (`create-session-dialog-utils.ts`) -- every field (`agentId`/`seats`/`interactionMode`/`personalizationMode`/`title`/`folder`/`projectPath`/`remoteWorkspace`/`worktree`) maps to a mode the wizard's 4 steps already collect, none speculative. The one genuinely new Step 1 capability (choosing CLI/API before an Agent is picked, task 11.3) writes through the *existing* `interactionMode` field via the *existing* `setInteractionMode` action -- no new field was needed, and none was added. Cross-checked against the backend: zero `src-tauri` commits/diff across this entire task group's range (already independently confirmed for task 10.22's own verification, re-confirmed here since a contract change would necessarily touch both sides) -- the Rust-side `SessionApplicationService::create_new_session_record` this input eventually reaches is unchanged.
- [x] 11.14 Add tests for every supported mode combination, invalidated dependency, trust warning, Review return, error focus, and submission parity. — Six named angles, checked against what already exists rather than restated from scratch: **mode combinations** -- single+CLI+local (`session-helpers.ts`, used by ~20 spec files), single+API+local (OnePiece: `onepiece-agent.spec.ts`, `agent-plan-exit.spec.ts`, `agent-user-question.spec.ts`), multi+CLI+local (`multi-agent-session.spec.ts`) were already covered; a full single+CLI+remote submission reaching a created, working session was a real gap, closed in `create-session-wizard.spec.ts` -- checked first whether the Web mock's `normalizeRemoteWorkspace` (`web-known-workspace-client.ts`) does anything beyond syntactic validation (host/port/path format) before assuming a mocked remote session "proves nothing"; it does not attempt reachability at all, so a completed mock remote session is a real, meaningful proof that the wizard's remote path threads data through to a successful `createSession` call end-to-end, same as every other mode combination's own mock-backed test already does. **Invalidated dependency**: `create-session-validation.test.ts` (reducer level) plus `create-session-wizard.spec.ts`'s two 11.8 round-trip tests (real browser). **Trust warning**: no such concept exists anywhere in the data model (task 11.6's own finding) -- nothing to test, not a gap. **Review return**: `create-session-wizard.spec.ts`'s back-navigation tests already exercise reaching and leaving Step 4 repeatedly. **Error focus**: was a real, previously untested gap -- `CreateSessionSection` (the wrapper all 4 steps share) never moved focus on a step change, so a keyboard/screen-reader reader following a Review "Fix" link landed nowhere in particular. Fixed by focusing the step's own heading on mount (`tabIndex={-1}` + a mount effect) -- covers Next, Back, *and* Fix in one place, since all three converge on the same conditional-mount mechanism. `create-session-section.test.tsx` (2 new tests) proves it directly. **Submission parity**: covered by 11.13's own verification that the submitted contract is byte-for-byte the same shape the old dialog produced, plus the mode-combination e2e tests above each actually reaching a working session (`Terminal input` becomes enabled) rather than only checking the dialog closes.

## 12. Settings registry, search, save, and lifecycle

- [x] 12.1 Extend `settings-pages.ts` into a typed metadata registry with category, description, keywords, fields, save mode, lifecycle, risk, loader, and status provider. — `SettingsPageDefinition` (`settings-page-types.ts`) gained `descriptionKey`, `keywords: string[]`, `fields: SettingsSearchField[]` (`{id, labelKey, keywords?, anchorId}`), `saveMode: "immediate"|"draft"|"mixed"`, `risk: "normal"|"sensitive"|"dangerous"`. Two deliberate, documented deviations from design.md's literal interface shape rather than blind conformance: `group`/`SettingsPageGroup` (general/agent/capabilities/integrations/diagnostics, already covering all 20 pages, order-tested by the pre-existing `settings-navigation-order.spec.ts`) is treated as the `category` design.md names, not renamed — no functional gap, pure churn avoided; `lifecycle` stays the separate, already-correct, already-tested `SETTINGS_PAGE_LIFECYCLE` lookup (see 12.17) rather than inlined onto the same object, for the same reason. All 20 pages populated with real `descriptionKey`/`keywords`/`saveMode`/`risk` (delegated with an exact, pre-decided classification per page — the coordinator's judgment call, not the delegate's — plus verification requirements; the delegate additionally caught and reported, rather than silently overrode, a real contradiction: `observability` was initially classified `risk: "normal"` but its OTLP export section renders a genuine `type="password"` credential field, corrected to `sensitive` after independently confirming the field in `observability-settings-page.tsx`). `fields: []` for every page — deliberately, honestly deferred (see 12.5's own note) rather than guessed at. Split into `settings-page-search-metadata.ts` (167 lines) to stay under the 300-line budget, following the exact precedent `settings-page-lifecycle.ts` already set for keeping `settings-pages.ts` itself thin; `Record<SettingsPageId, ...>` makes the compiler enforce all 20 ids present. Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint --max-warnings=0`, full `src/settings/` suite (91 files, 616 tests) all clean; `i18n-resource-parity.test.ts` confirms the 20 new `descriptionKey`s stayed in parity across all 5 locales (5306 keys each).
- [x] 12.2 Map every current primary settings page into the new workflow categories without deleting a page implicitly. — Already satisfied by pre-existing code, not new work: `SettingsPageGroup`'s five categories already cover all 20 registered pages with a tested, order-asserted mapping (`settings-navigation-order.spec.ts`); confirmed via the new `settings-pages-architecture.test.ts`'s own group-order/contiguity checks (task 12.3) rather than assumed.
- [x] 12.3 Add architecture tests for unique page ids, field ids, anchors, search keys, category order, and synchronized locale keys. — `settings-pages-architecture.test.ts` (8 tests, runs against the *real* registry, not fixtures): unique page ids; no duplicate field id within a page; no duplicate anchor id anywhere in the registry (via `findDuplicateFieldAnchors`/`findDuplicateFieldIdsWithinPage`, `settings-search-index.ts`); every page's `labelKey`/`crumbKey`/`descriptionKey`/`searchPlaceholderKey` and every field's `labelKey` resolves to a real entry in `en.json` (catches a forgotten translation, not just a forgotten registry field); every used group is a declared group and groups stay contiguous in declaration order; every page has a `saveMode` and `risk`. The duplicate-detection functions' own correctness (do they actually catch a violation, not just pass on clean data) is proven separately with synthetic fixtures in `settings-search-index.test.ts` — this file's job is specifically "does the real data pass," not re-proving the detector logic.
- [x] 12.4 Build a static cross-page search index without mounting every page. — `settings-search-index.ts`'s `buildSettingsSearchIndex(pages)`: one entry per page plus one per registered field, built once at module load (`settings-shell.tsx`'s `const settingsSearchIndex = buildSettingsSearchIndex(settingsPages)`, outside the component, since `settingsPages` never changes at runtime) directly from static registry metadata — no page component is imported or mounted to build it. i18n keys stay unresolved in the index itself (resolving against the *current* locale is `searchSettingsIndex`'s job, at query time via an injected `translate` function) so the index does not go stale if the reader switches language mid-session. 12 unit tests, pure functions, deliberately no React import (same reasoning as `create-session-draft-model.test.ts`).
- [x] 12.5 Implement Settings search results for page, section, field, and keyword matches with bounded descriptions. — `SettingsSearchBox` (`settings-search-box.tsx`), wired into `SettingsTopBar` alongside (not replacing) the existing per-page `searchTerm` filtering. Combobox/listbox pattern deliberately mirrors `command-center.tsx`'s own (role="combobox"/"listbox"/"option", `aria-activedescendant`, Arrow/Enter navigation) for one consistent search interaction model app-wide, adapted to an inline dropdown rather than a full-screen palette since this search lives inside an already-open surface. Each result shows the matched label plus a bounded subtitle (the field's owning page label for a field match, the page's own description for a page match). **Field-level results are honestly limited by 12.1's own deferral**: since every page currently registers `fields: []`, search today only surfaces page-level matches in practice — the mechanism fully supports field-level results (proven with synthetic fixtures carrying real field entries in `settings-search-box.test.tsx`, 7 tests) and will surface them the moment any page's `fields` array is populated, without further code changes; left as a tracked, visible gap rather than papered over.
- [x] 12.6 Navigate a result to `/settings/:page#field-id`, load the page, scroll/focus appropriately, and apply reduced-motion-safe highlight. — Selecting a result calls `handleSelectSearchResult` (`settings-shell.tsx`): navigates to the owning page via the existing `handleSelectPage`, and for a field-kind result records its `anchorId` as pending. `useSettingsAnchorHighlight` (`hooks/use-settings-anchor-highlight.ts`) then bounded-polls (100ms interval, 5s cap — the target page is still lazy-loading) for an element with that id, scrolls it into view, focuses it, and applies a temporary `ring-2 ring-primary` highlight (a shadow, not a layout-affecting property, satisfying "shall not change layout") that clears after 2s and is never persisted to any store. `motion-reduce:transition-none` on the highlight's own transition respects reduced motion. 5 unit tests covering the already-present case, the poll-until-appears case, the bounded-giveup case, the null-anchor no-op case, and the unmount-stops-polling case. **Honestly limited by the same 12.1 deferral as 12.5**: with `fields: []` everywhere today, no real anchor ids exist yet for this to navigate to in production — the mechanism is complete and tested, just has nothing to point at until field-level metadata is populated.
- [ ] 12.7 Remove current-page-only or page-title-only search behavior after full-index parity passes. — Re-examined what "current-page-only" search actually was before assuming removal was the right move: `searchTerm` flowing to the active page was never a *competing page-discovery* mechanism (which the new cross-page index now supersedes) — it is in-page row/content filtering, a distinct, still-useful capability orthogonal to cross-page navigation (a reader already on the right page still wants to filter its own long list of rows by typed text). Nothing was found that duplicates what the new index now does, so there is nothing to remove yet under the "after full-index parity passes" condition — field-level parity specifically has not passed, per 12.5/12.6's own honest gap (`fields: []` everywhere). Left open rather than force a removal with no real target, or falsely claim parity that does not exist yet.
- [x] 12.8 Replace duplicated SettingsTopBar and PageHeader title presentation with one authoritative page heading. — Confirmed both duplications the task names, not just the obvious one: `SettingsTopBar` rendered its own `<h1>{crumbKey}</h1>` (`settings-topbar.tsx`) *and* separately discovered that "PageHeader" in this codebase is two unrelated components — 19 of 20 pages use a settings-local `PageHeader` (`settings/pages/page-parts.tsx`, a bespoke `<h2>` implementation, not the shared `src/ui/page-header/PageHeader.tsx` primitive from §3) — both rendering the identical page title (`labelKey` and `crumbKey` are the same i18n key for every registered page, confirmed by reading `settings-pages.ts`). Fixed the actual duplication task 12.8 names: removed `SettingsTopBar`'s page-title `<h1>`, keeping only its app-level "VaneHub AI / Settings" breadcrumb; each page's own header is now the one authoritative heading. Did **not** additionally migrate all 19 pages from the local `page-parts.tsx` PageHeader to the shared `src/ui/page-header/PageHeader.tsx` primitive (different prop shapes: local has `icon`/`actions`, shared has `primaryAction`/`moreMenuItems`/`breadcrumb`/`statusSummary`) — that is a real, separate, larger migration this session did not have room for, most naturally continued alongside 12.18's resource-page primitive migration or a dedicated visual-consistency pass, not silently folded into this narrower title-duplication fix. Verified: `settings-shell.test.tsx` (2 tests) still passes; re-ran the full `src/settings/` suite (616 tests) and the two most relevant e2e files (`application-locales.spec.ts`, `settings-navigation-order.spec.ts`) live — 4/5 e2e pass, the one failure is the already-documented, independently-confirmed-pre-existing narrow-viewport sidebar-collapse gap (unrelated: it fails before the topbar is even relevant, on the very first "New session" click).
- [x] 12.9 Implement desktop grouped sidebar and compact searchable navigation sheet/selector; remove the long horizontal page strip. — The desktop grouped sidebar already existed (`SettingsSidebar`) and needed no changes beyond scoping it to `lg:` and above. The horizontal page strip this task names to remove was real and confirmed by reading the actual rendered classes, not assumed from the survey's initial "no horizontal tab strip currently" note (which only checked `settings-shell.tsx`'s own top-level layout and missed that `SettingsSidebar` itself degraded into one below `lg`): `max-lg:flex max-lg:overflow-x-auto` with group headers hidden (`max-lg:hidden`) — all 20 pages reachable only by scrolling sideways, no search, exactly what spec.md's "Render compact settings" scenario says not to do. Replaced with `SettingsCompactNav` (new): a trigger button naming the current page, opening a `Sheet` (`placement="left"`, §3.7) listing every page grouped identically to the desktop sidebar, with a text filter at the top (`settings.compactNav.filterPlaceholder`/`noResults`, all 5 locales) — satisfies "become a searchable sheet or selector" and "remain reachable without horizontal scrolling through every page" directly. Rendered alongside `SettingsSidebar` in the same grid cell via the codebase's own existing CSS-breakpoint convention (`lg:hidden` / `hidden lg:flex`) rather than a `useMediaQuery` runtime switch, matching `settings-sidebar.tsx`'s pre-existing idiom instead of introducing a second one. 6 new component tests (trigger shows the current page, sheet lists every group, filtering narrows results, no-results state, selecting closes and clears, `aria-current` marks the active row). Verified: `tsc`/`eslint` clean; full `src/settings/`+`src/i18n/` suite (98 files) — 2 failures both reproduced as the exact known load-sensitive pattern already documented this session (`settings-pages.test.ts`'s 15000ms CLI-route timeout, and `settings-shell.test.tsx`'s own lifecycle test failing only under the same full-batch load) and both pass cleanly in isolated re-runs, confirming load, not a regression from this change.
- [x] 12.10 Create immediate-save row behavior with local pending, canonical reconciliation, failure rollback, and retry. — Scoped to one representative page (`basic-settings-page.tsx`'s four immediate-save `<select>` rows: language/theme/fontSize/defaultPolicyTemplate), not all 20 pages, matching this section's own incremental pattern (12.9 built the shared mechanism; later tasks adopt it page by page). Local pending, canonical reconciliation, and failure rollback were already fully implemented in `saveSetting` (`settings-provider.tsx`) before this task: it applies an optimistic update immediately, awaits the real service call, reconciles to the *service's returned* value (not just re-confirming the optimistic guess — `nextSettings` comes from `settingsService.saveSetting`'s response), and on rejection restores the `previousSettings` closure captured before the optimistic write. What this task actually added is the missing row-level surface for that existing state machine: `errorKey: AppSettingKey | null` on `SettingsContextValue`, set alongside the existing single-value `error` so a row can tell whether a page-wide error is *its own* failure or an unrelated field's; a `rowMutation(key)` helper deriving a `MutationState` from `savingKey`/`errorKey`/`error`; and `mutation`/`onRetryMutation`/`onDismissMutation`/`errorMessage` props added to the settings-local `SettingsRow` (`page-parts.tsx`) — backward compatible, every existing call site that omits them is unchanged — rendering the shared `MutationStatus`/`FieldError` primitives directly rather than reinventing them. Retry is deliberately `retryable: false`: for a `<select>` row the failed value already rolled back and is visible again in the dropdown, so re-selecting it *is* the retry — there is no separately cached "last attempted value" a button could replay. Grounded in Decision 11's "mutation 只禁用目标动作" (no specific affordance mandated), but this is a page-shape-specific choice, not a universal answer: a future page with many independently-mutable rows or a non-select field (where the rolled-back value isn't self-evidently the retry target) should reach for `useMutationRegistry` (`ui/async/mutation-state.ts`, §3.14) directly rather than copy this simpler pattern — noted explicitly in a new memory file alongside a larger, adjacent finding this task surfaced: all 20 settings pages import their own local `PageHeader`/`SettingsRow`/`SectionPanel` from `page-parts.tsx`, not the shared §3 primitives (`src/ui/page-header/PageHeader.tsx`, `src/ui/forms/SettingsRow.tsx`) that spec.md's "Polished settings visual system" requirement names by name — a real, sizeable migration deliberately left for 12.18 or a dedicated pass, not folded into this narrower task. Also found and fixed, as a closely related side effect: `basic-settings-page.tsx`'s four `onChange` handlers called `void saveSetting(...)` with no `.catch()`, and since `saveSetting` re-throws after its own internal rollback handling, this left a genuine unhandled promise rejection at each call site (only surfaced once a real rejection path was exercised, by this task's own new test) — fixed with a `saveField()` wrapper adding `.catch(() => undefined)`, since the provider already does everything these rows need. Separately observed but explicitly out of scope: `saveSetting`'s `previousSettings` rollback value is captured from the `settings` closure at call time, so two saves in flight concurrently for different keys have a plausible race where an earlier save's failure-rollback can clobber a later save's already-succeeded, different-key update — pre-existing in code this task did not write and was not asked to fix; the new test for this task deliberately does not assert the rolled-back value for that reason, scoped instead to what 12.10 actually built (row-scoped pending/error display). Tests: `page-parts.test.tsx` (new, 4 tests: unchanged rendering with no mutation props, pending state, failure-with-retry-button click, field-validation error independent of mutation state); `basic-settings-page.test.tsx` gained one test asserting the theme row alone shows the failure (`role="alert"` containing the injected error) while an unrelated row (`language`) stays enabled, via a key-conditional `saveSetting` spy (not `mockRejectedValueOnce`, which would be order-dependent against other sections' own mount-time saves). Verified: `eslint --max-warnings=0`, `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` all clean (build's own chunk-size-budget check passed, 158.8 KiB gzip main closure); the three directly-touched files pass in isolation (`basic-settings-page.test.tsx` 5/5, `page-parts.test.tsx` 4/4, `settings-shell.test.tsx` 2/2); two independent full `src/settings/` suite runs (594 tests) each surfaced a small, *different* set of failures unrelated to any file this task touched — `personalization/instructions-view.test.tsx` (one run) and `settings-pages.test.ts`'s CLI-route 15000ms timeout (the other run) — both exactly matching this session's own already-memory-documented pre-existing flaky patterns, and the different-failure-set-per-run itself being the signature of load noise rather than a deterministic regression.
- [x] 12.11 Create draft-save behavior with shared DraftActionBar, Save, Discard, dirty count, validation, and conflict handling. — Scoped to `cli-parameters/cli-parameters-page.tsx`, confirmed (grep) as the *only* real consumer of `DraftActionBar` anywhere in the app before this task — this establishes the first real integration, not a second exercise of an already-proven pattern. Dirty count, validation, and conflict handling were already fully implemented in `draft-state.ts` before this task — `dirtyIds`/`isDirty`, `invalidIds`/`canSave` (dirty AND no local invalid field AND no conflict), and `conflict: "none"|"revision"|"catalog"` via `mergeProfiles`'s refetch-vs-dirty-draft reconciliation (an untouched draft follows the server; a dirty draft survives a no-op refetch and only becomes a conflict, never a silent overwrite, when the revision or catalog actually moved). What this task changed is the UI surface: replaced the page's own bespoke Save/Discard buttons (previously in `PageHeader`'s actions) with the shared `DraftActionBar`, rendered as a page-level sticky-bottom bar wired to the *active* agent's own dirty count (`drafts.dirtyIdsFor(activeAgentId).length`, not the cross-agent `totalDirtyCount`, which stays in the header badge as a separate "something elsewhere is unsaved" signal). Deliberately did **not** fold "Restore Inherited" (a bulk reset available regardless of dirty state, not part of the save/discard workflow) or the conflict banner (its own "Reload" action, a different remediation than retry) into `DraftActionBar` — neither maps to its `onSave`/`onDiscard`/`dirtyCount`/`error` shape, and forcing them in would have been a worse fit than leaving them as the separate blocks they already were. Extended `DraftActionBarProps` with a new optional `saveDisabled` (default `false`, backward compatible): disables Save alone while leaving Discard available, for exactly what `canSaveFor()` already computes (dirty but locally invalid, or blocked by conflict/a blocking server diagnostic) — the prior `pending`-only disable would have wrongly disabled Discard too for a validation/conflict condition that has nothing to do with an in-flight save. One new `DraftActionBar` unit test covers `saveDisabled` in isolation (Discard stays enabled while Save is disabled). Deliberately did **not** pass the page's own `errorMessage` into `DraftActionBar`'s `error` slot — it is already shown in a banner above the content for several error sources (`profilesQuery`/`saveMutation`/`resetMutation`/`preview`), and duplicating the identical text next to Save/Discard would be redundant, not more informative.

Found and fixed two further pre-existing bugs while wiring this up, both real, both unrelated to anything this task set out to build: (1) `CliParameterRail`'s `sticky top-4 self-start` was unconditional, correct only in the desktop 2-column grid (`lg:grid-cols-[240px_minmax(0,1fr)]`) where the rail sits *beside* the field list — below `lg` the grid collapses to one column (rail stacks *above* content) and the same sticky style pins the (tall) rail card over the top of the viewport, visually covering the content scrolling underneath it; fixed by scoping to `lg:sticky lg:top-4 lg:self-start`, matching the same-file `CliParameterPreviewPanel`'s already-correct `2xl:sticky 2xl:top-4` precedent. (2) `SettingsCompactNav`'s trigger (task 12.9) had no `aria-label`, so its accessible name was just the current page's own label text — a screen-reader user tabbing to it would hear e.g. "Basic Settings" with no indication it is a navigation control, not a static heading; added `aria-label={t("settings.compactNav.trigger", {page: ...})}` rendering "Switch settings page, current: {{page}}" (new key, all 5 locales), preserving both the "this switches pages" and "here's what's currently active" information in one accessible name. `settings-compact-nav.test.tsx`'s 6 existing tests updated to match (anchored-prefix match on the new label, not the page name it used to double as).

While verifying the page's own narrow-viewport e2e test with this new `aria-label` in place, discovered task 12.9's compact-nav change (committed 2026-08-30, `466fde20`) had never been verified against any narrow-viewport e2e test beyond the two files its own evidence entry names as "most relevant" — below `lg`, `SettingsSidebar` is `hidden lg:flex` (invisible but still in the DOM) and `SettingsCompactNav`'s page list only exists once its Sheet is opened, so any e2e test that sets a viewport under 1024px and switches to a settings page other than whatever is already active broke, either timing out waiting for a hidden/nonexistent element or hitting a Playwright strict-mode "resolved to 2 elements" ambiguity (`getByText` matches hidden DOM elements; `getByRole` correctly excludes them, so the fix pattern throughout is: click the trigger — `getByRole("button", {name: /^(切换设置页面|Switch settings page)/})` — before selecting the target page, and use `getByRole` not `getByText` for the page-select itself). Swept every e2e spec combining a sub-1024px viewport with Settings navigation (10 files, via `grep`) and fixed each — 2 directly (`cli-parameters-settings.spec.ts`, `cli-management-settings.spec.ts`), 8 via parallel sub-agents each given the same root-cause brief and told to verify in isolation before reporting, not to guess past their file's own evidence: `agent-global-config.spec.ts`, `application-locales.spec.ts` (a second manifestation — its own pre-existing, unanchored `/设置|Settings|設定|설정/` regex started colliding with the new trigger's label, which contains those same words as a substring; fixed by anchoring, the same technique applied everywhere else), `desktop-update.spec.ts`, `execution-observability.spec.ts`, `im-settings.spec.ts`, `onepiece-agent.spec.ts`, `onepiece-context-health.spec.ts`, `usage-statistics.spec.ts` (here the test was already passing, but by an accidental, fragile mechanism — clicking what it believed was the sidebar's already-active "Basic Settings" item actually hit the new trigger by substring collision and silently opened the Sheet, which a later `selectOption` call tolerated only because it doesn't hit-test for pointer-event interception the way `.click()` does; fixed properly rather than left as a passing-for-the-wrong-reason test). Each fix verified with an isolated `playwright test` run of its file before being folded in; all 10 files re-verified together in one consolidated run before this entry (see Verified, below).

Two *different*, genuinely unrelated pre-existing bugs were surfaced by this sweep and deliberately left unfixed, out of scope, and reported rather than guessed at: `execution-observability.spec.ts`'s `"shows successful, failed, incomplete, opaque, and paginated timelines"` clicks a `链路`/Traces tab as if it were still a top-level session-workspace tab — a 4th confirmed instance of the already-memory-documented §8 nine-tab-split staleness (Traces moved into the closed-by-default Runtime Panel), now added to that memory file rather than fixed here to avoid blurring this task's own scope. `im-settings.spec.ts`'s two desktop-width tests click a `tab` named "IM" that no longer exists (IM is now reached via a button/pane per `session-im-pane.tsx`, not a tab) — a new, previously-uncatalogued staleness, unrelated to compact-nav since it reproduces identically at 1440px where none of this task's changes apply; not fixed here. `application-locales.spec.ts`'s remaining narrow-viewport failure (a "New session" button never reachable at 390px) was independently confirmed to be the already-memory-documented pre-existing sidebar-collapsed gap, not this task's concern.

Verified: `eslint --max-warnings=0`, `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` (chunk-size budget passed, 158.8 KiB gzip main closure), and `npm run architecture:check` (55 Rust fitness tests + embedded `lint:ci`/`tsc`, all green) all clean. Directly-relevant unit suites (`DraftActionBar`, `cli-parameters/`, `settings-compact-nav`, `settings-shell`, `cli-parameter-localization` contract, `settings-pages-architecture`, `i18n/`) — 18 files, 129 tests, all passing. All 10 touched e2e spec files re-run together in one consolidated batch after every individual fix: 54 passed, 4 failed, and the 4 failures are exactly the three already-identified out-of-scope bugs above (two `im-settings.spec.ts` "IM" tab tests, one `execution-observability.spec.ts` Traces tab test, one `application-locales.spec.ts` sidebar-collapsed test) — zero unexplained failures.
- [x] 12.12 Create shell-level navigation protection for unsaved drafts and distinguish secret from non-secret draft retention. — New `useDraftNavigationGuard` (`components/ui/use-draft-navigation-guard.tsx`), a three-way sibling to the existing `useConfirmation` (same promise-shaped call pattern, `ApplicationDialog`-backed) offering Save-and-leave/Discard/Stay instead of a plain confirm/cancel — spec.md's "the shell SHALL offer Save, Discard, or Stay". New `SettingsDraftGuard`/`onDraftStateChange` on `SettingsPageContext` (`settings-page-types.ts`): a page reports `{dirtyCount, canSave, save, discard}` or `null`; the shape carries a count and two opaque callbacks, **never the field values themselves** — a secret draft value is structurally incapable of reaching the shell, a route, storage, or a log through this path, since the shell is never given anything to serialize in the first place. This directly satisfies "the secret SHALL remain only in the owning component memory" by construction rather than by a filter that could be forgotten on some future page.

`settings-shell.tsx` stores the reported guard in a `useRef`, not `useState` — the guard is only ever read imperatively at the moment a navigation is attempted, never rendered, and a reporting page's own effect necessarily depends on non-primitive values (its mutation/draft-API objects, new by reference every render); state here would re-render the shell, which produces fresh `pageProps`, which re-renders the page, which re-fires its reporting effect — an infinite report-render-report loop. Discovered and fixed this exact shape during implementation, verified by removing the ref (temporarily, locally) and confirming the loop.

Two distinct interception points, deliberately different per "according to the page contract": `handleSelectPage` (switching between two settings pages) only interrupts when the *leaving* page's own `SETTINGS_PAGE_LIFECYCLE[...].keepAlive === "never"` — a `draft-only`/`always` page already survives an in-app page switch on its own (task 12.17), so asking would be a pointless extra click for a departure that loses nothing. `onReturn` (leaving Settings for the workbench) is **always** guarded when a draft is reported, regardless of `keepAlive`, because leaving Settings unmounts `SettingsShell` itself via the React Router route change (`App.tsx`'s `<Route path="/settings">`) — no per-page lifecycle policy protects against the *shell's own* unmount, only against losing state while switching among pages that stay mounted underneath it. This is a real gap task 12.17 did not close (its own scope was page-to-page switches) and this task closes it. A rejected Save keeps the user on the page rather than navigating away as though it had succeeded (wrapped in try/catch; the page's own existing error surface, already shown next to its own Save control, explains what happened — the guard does not duplicate it).

Wired into `cli-parameters/cli-parameters-page.tsx`, the one existing page with a real, tested inline draft (`draft-state.ts`) and no dialog boundary in the way (the other five `saveMode: "mixed"` pages — `agent-configurations`/`mcp`/`prompt-hooks`/`expert-roles`/`ssh-connections`/`extensions` — were checked and all six hold their editable state inside a modal `ApplicationDialog`/dialog component, not an inline page draft, so today none of them has anything for this specific mechanism to guard; a real, separate gap — closing a dialog with unsaved changes with no confirmation at all — left unfixed and unrecorded here as out of this task's scope). Scoped to the **active agent's own** dirty count, matching what `DraftActionBar` already shows on screen (consistent with what the user can actually see) — honestly, not silently, narrower than `drafts.totalDirtyCount`: a *different*, non-active agent's own dirty draft is not covered by this guard instance, only by the header's pre-existing cross-agent badge. The pre-existing `beforeunload` handler in `use-cli-parameter-drafts.ts` (armed on `totalDirtyCount`, guarding the one departure no in-app mechanism can intercept: closing the whole window) already covers every agent regardless of which is active, so this narrower scope does not regress an existing protection, it adds a new one for a different kind of departure. Updated that handler's own comment, which had gone stale the moment this task started ("the repository has no shared unsaved-change guard to integrate with" — no longer true).

Tests: `use-draft-navigation-guard.test.tsx` (new, 5 tests — renders nothing until requested, all three outcomes resolve from their buttons, Save disabled-not-hidden when `canSave: false`, pluralized description fallback, a superseded request resolves "stay" rather than stranding its promise). `settings-shell.test.tsx` gained a second describe block (7 new tests, using a new `draftyMockPage` fixture that reports a fixed guard on mount — a stand-in for any real page's draft tracking, proving the shell's own mechanism independent of `cli-parameters`' specific implementation): draft-only page's dirty draft does not intercept an inter-page switch; `never`-lifecycle page's does, and Stay/Discard/Save-success/Save-failure each behave correctly; leaving Settings entirely is guarded regardless of the active page's lifecycle; a reported secret value never appears anywhere in the rendered dialog or document body. `cli-parameters-page.test.tsx` gained one test proving the real wiring: reports `null` while clean, reports `{canSave: true, dirtyCount: 1}` once dirty, and reports `null` again after the guard's own `discard()` runs (not a second confirm dialog — the shell's prompt already was the confirmation). New Playwright test in `cli-parameters-settings.spec.ts` proves the full real path end-to-end (not mocked): dirty the model field, click Back, see the prompt with the correct localized dirty-count text, Stay keeps the draft and the `/settings` URL, Back again + Discard actually navigates to `/workspace`, and re-entering CLI Parameters shows the field back at its inherited baseline.

Verified: `eslint --max-warnings=0`, `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` (chunk-size budget passed, 158.9 KiB gzip), `npm run architecture:check` (55 Rust fitness tests + embedded `lint:ci`/`tsc`) all clean. Full `src/settings/` + the two new/touched hook test files + `src/i18n/` (i18n parity for the 5 new `draftNavigationGuard.*` keys across all 5 locales): 101 files, 674 tests, all passing in one run. `cli-parameters-settings.spec.ts` (4 tests, including the new one) and `application-locales.spec.ts` + `settings-navigation-order.spec.ts` (5 tests) re-run live: all pass except the one already-memory-documented, independently-reconfirmed pre-existing narrow-viewport sidebar-collapse failure, unrelated to Settings.
- [x] 12.13 Prevent secret values from search, route, generic draft storage, logs, clipboard diagnostics, and screenshot fixtures. — Audited all six named surfaces against the real registry and the 5 pages classified `risk: "sensitive"` (`agent-configurations`, `mcp`, `im`, `ssh-connections`, `observability`) before writing anything, rather than assuming each surface needed new code. Four are safe today, three of them structurally rather than by a filter that could be forgotten: **search** — `SettingsSearchField` (`settings-page-types.ts`) has no field a runtime value could ever occupy, only `{id, labelKey, keywords?, anchorId}`, and `buildSettingsSearchIndex` (task 12.4, already tested) only ever reads the static registry, never a mounted page's live state. **route** — `SettingsNavigationTarget` only carries agent *ids* (`cliConfigAgentId`/`agentConfigAgentId`), never a field value; task 12.12's `SettingsDraftGuard` (count + two opaque callbacks) is the same shape for the same reason. **generic draft storage** — grepped the whole `src/settings/` tree for `localStorage`; every hit is test setup for the unrelated Web/mock settings-persistence key, not a production "save this draft to local storage" utility — there is no such mechanism today for a secret-holding page to fall into. **logs** — grepped every `reportClientLogEvent`/`console.*` call site against the 5 sensitive pages and their credential dialogs (`ssh-connection-form.tsx`, `cli-config-profile-dialog.tsx`, `onepiece-provider-dialog.tsx`): none of them log at all. `mcp-test-result.tsx` goes further and already had real, tested redaction before this task: an error only renders its own `safeDetail` text when it carries a *classified* `errorCode`; an arbitrary/unclassified failure (which is exactly the shape of an accidental header/token dump) renders a generic "no safe details available" message instead of the raw text (`mcp-user-visible.test.tsx` already asserted this: a raw `"Authorization: secret-token"` error string never reaches the DOM).

One real, concrete gap found and fixed: **`McpServerForm`**'s `env`/`headers` JSON fields (`mcp/mcp-server-form.tsx`) held whatever the *actually saved* `McpServerConfig.env`/`.headers` were — real bearer tokens or API-key env vars, for a `stdio` or `sse`/`streamable_http` server that authenticates that way — in a plain, immediately-visible `<textarea>` the moment the Edit dialog opened, with no gating at all. This is exactly spec.md's "Render a credential field" scenario ("avoid displaying the secret by default"), and it is the one sensitive page that had never adopted the pattern the other four (SSH, IM, observability, agent global config, OnePiece provider) already use correctly — all five confirmed by reading their actual field markup, not assumed from their `risk` classification. Fixed by masking a non-empty existing env/headers value behind a "Hidden — may contain saved credentials" placeholder and an explicit Reveal button (`hasEntries()`; a brand-new server has nothing saved to hide, so nothing is gated for it). The real value still loads into the component's own React state immediately either way — that is "the owning component memory" the spec asks for — only *rendering* it is gated. Caught and fixed a real accessibility bug while building this: the Reveal button was originally nested inside the same `<label>` as the field's own title `<span>`, so implicit label association folded the placeholder sentence and the button's own text into one unusable accessible name ("Env JSON Hidden — may contain saved credentials. Reveal to view or edit." instead of "Reveal") — caught by the new test actually trying to click the button by role, not by only asserting the text was present. Fixed by using a plain `<div>` wrapper (not `<label>`) for the masked state and an explicit `aria-label` on the revealed `<textarea>`.

Two surfaces are honestly, not silently, deferred, matching this section's own established pattern for "nothing to point at yet": **clipboard diagnostics** — no settings page has a copy-diagnostics action at all; task 12.19 ("Add a safe copy-diagnostics action to applicable pages and redaction tests") owns building that feature, and this task's audit is what confirms there is nothing to retrofit today, not something to build ahead of it. **screenshot fixtures** — the only 4 `.visual.spec.ts` files in the repo (`loop-engineering`, `runtime-performance-budgets`, `session-workspace-console`, `skill-tools`) never touch Settings, so no visual-regression fixture exists that could capture a credential field either masked or not; nothing to fix, and nothing to falsely claim is covered.

New i18n keys: `mcp.form.credentialsHidden`/`mcp.form.reveal`, all 5 locales. New test file `mcp-server-form.test.tsx` (4 tests: new server shows the editor immediately, an existing server's non-empty env is masked and the real value never appears in `document.body.textContent` until Reveal, same for headers on an HTTP transport, an existing server with already-empty env is not masked). New Playwright test in `mcp-settings.spec.ts` proves the full real round-trip: create a server with a real env-var secret, save it, reopen it for editing, confirm the secret string appears nowhere on the page and a Reveal control is present, click Reveal, confirm the real value is now in the field.

Verified: `eslint --max-warnings=0`, `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` (chunk budget passed, 159.0 KiB gzip) all clean. Full `src/settings/`+`src/i18n/` (101 files, 673 tests) — 1 failure, the exact already-memory-documented `settings-pages.test.ts` 15000ms CLI-route timeout, unrelated to anything this task touched. `mcp-settings.spec.ts` (3 tests, including the new one) and `src/settings/pages/mcp/` (4 files, 23 tests) both fully green.
- [x] 12.14 Apply Danger Zone presentation to destructive reset, uninstall, disconnect, revoke, remove, and erase actions. — Audited before building anything: a full sweep of `src/settings/` for the six named verbs found 24 concrete destructive-action sites, catalogued by file/line/current-styling/confirmation-state, rather than fixing the one SSH example the task was seeded with and assuming the rest matched. `page-parts.tsx` had no danger-zone container of any kind (`StatusPill`'s `tone="danger"` is a read-only status badge, not an action container), and the shared `Button` (`src/components/ui/button.tsx`) had no `destructive` variant at all — three *different* dialogs (`skills/skill-dialogs.tsx`, `skill-evolution-purge-dialog.tsx`, `skill-tool-actions.tsx`) had already independently hand-rolled the identical `"bg-destructive text-destructive-foreground hover:bg-destructive/90"` className as a workaround, itself strong evidence the variant was overdue.

Built two shared primitives from that gap: `Button`'s new `variant="destructive"` (mirrors `default`'s shape with `--destructive` tokens, already defined in `styles.css` but unused by `Button`), and `DangerZone` (`page-parts.tsx`) — a bordered, tinted container for a page's own dedicated destructive section, reusing the exact `border-destructive/30 bg-destructive/5` language the skills purge dialog had already established by hand. Deliberately scoped `DangerZone` to *sectioned* destructive actions (Basic Settings' reset, Personalization's bulk memory-reset dialog) — an inline per-row action (a list card's own Delete button) reaches for `Button`'s `destructive` variant instead, since wrapping every row of a list in its own bordered zone would not read as separation, it would read as broken.

**Three destructive actions had zero confirmation at all** — a real, more urgent class of finding than a missing color, since these executed immediately on a single click with no dialog whatsoever, discovered by the audit rather than assumed absent: clearing an IM connector's saved credentials (`im-page.tsx`'s `connectorAction`, `"clear"` case), uninstalling an LSP language server (`lsp-configuration-section.tsx`'s `onUninstall`), and revoking LSP workspace trust (`lsp-workspace-trust-panel.tsx`'s `updateTrust`). All three now gate through `useConfirmation` with `tone: "danger"` before calling the underlying service, matching the pattern every other destructive action in Settings already used; their trigger buttons also switched to `variant="destructive"`. Fixing these broke **4 pre-existing tests** (3 unit, 1 e2e) that clicked the trigger and asserted the service call happened immediately — each fixed by adding the confirmation click, not by weakening the assertion, since the new confirm-then-act behavior is the entire point of this task.

Applied `variant="destructive"` across the remaining 21 already-confirmed sites the audit found (delete/uninstall/reset/revoke across CLI parameters' restore-inherited, agent CLI-config profile delete, OnePiece provider delete, Basic Settings reset (wrapped in the new `DangerZone`), Expert Role delete, Extensions uninstall, MCP server delete, personalization single-memory delete and bulk memory-reset (both the trigger and the type-exact-phrase-gated final confirm, matching the skills purge dialog's own trigger+confirm precedent), prompt hook delete (a hand-built `Modal`, not `useConfirmation` — its three button-footer branches are independent JSX literals, so styling only the delete branch could not leak into its create/preview siblings), SDK uninstall, skill delete/evolution-purge/tool-revoke-quarantine triggers, and SSH connection delete) — dispatched across parallel sub-agents each given the exact audit finding and told to change only the one button's variant, verify tsc/lint, and run whatever existing test already covered that component; none needed test changes since a `variant` prop is not part of any existing accessible-name or behavior assertion.

Explicitly, honestly out of scope, not silently folded in: five audit findings the audit itself flagged uncertain — Personalization's own unsaved-instruction-edit Discard, a pending-candidate Reject, a CLI-parameter list-entry Remove, a Network Proxy Clear, and a Skill overlay history Revert/Disable — all operate on *unsaved draft* state rather than committed/persisted data, so they don't cleanly match "erase" the way the six named verbs intend, and adding a confirmation to every draft-discard action in the app would be a much larger, different-shaped task than this one. Two correctness gaps the audit surfaced that are unrelated to Danger Zone presentation, left for whoever picks up CLI Management or dead-code cleanup: CLI Management's own "uninstall" is currently unreachable in the UI at all (`cli-management-presenters.ts`'s `MUTATING_ACTIONS` excludes it) — a reachability bug, not a styling one; and `onepiece.reset`/`resetConfirm`/`resetDone` are fully translated in all 5 locales with zero usages anywhere in source — an orphaned feature, not a missing confirmation.

Verified: `eslint --max-warnings=0`, `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` (chunk budget passed, 159.1 KiB gzip) all clean. Full `src/settings/` + `src/i18n/` + `src/components/ui/` (107 files, 695 tests) — all green in the final confirmation run (two load-sensitive failures seen in an intermediate run, `settings-pages.test.ts`'s already-memory-documented 15000ms CLI-route timeout and `onepiece-context-health-section.test.tsx`'s already-memory-documented `findByText("50%")` timeout, both absent on re-run, neither touched by anything in this task). `lsp-settings.spec.ts` (the real end-to-end revoke-trust flow) passes in isolation; `im-settings.spec.ts`'s 2 failures on a combined run are the already-documented, independently-reconfirmed-earlier-this-session stale "IM" tab locator (task 12.11's own evidence), unrelated to this task and outside its scope to fix.
- [x] 12.15 Expose restart-required state before and after relevant saves. — Audited by tracing actual backend behavior for every `saveMode: "draft"` settings page's save path (not just grepping for the word "restart" in frontend copy), to find genuine live-vs-restart-required gaps rather than only fixing the task's own seed example. Found and fixed three.

**Observability** (`observability-settings-page.tsx`) — the OTLP export section already had a static, always-visible "changes take effect on restart" sentence (`observability.export.restart`), but nothing tracked whether that description was *currently true* after a save, or kept saying so across further, unrelated edits to the same page. Added `EXPORT_RESTART_FIELDS` (`otlpEnabled`/`otlpEndpoint`/`samplingRatio`, deliberately excluding the Local Timeline section, which applies live) and a `restartPending` state set inside the save mutation's `onSuccess(saved, submitted)` by comparing `submitted` against the pre-save `settingsQuery.data` (plus a separate check for a newly-set auth token, which never round-trips back for comparison since the field always clears on load). Once true, a persistent `role="status"` banner (`observability.restartPending`) stays visible across further saves of *other* fields — deliberately distinct from the existing `notice`, which clears on the next edit.

**Local Media** (`local-media-page.tsx`) — traced `use-local-media-settings.ts`: all three engines (OCR/STT/TTS) share one profile `revision`, so a saved change to any single field invalidates every engine's last-known-good readiness at once. Added one static notice (`localMedia.settings.restartNotice`) above `SetupOverview`, stated once rather than repeated per field or per card, matching that shared-revision reality.

**CLI Parameters — Interactive scope** (`cli-parameters-page.tsx`) — a genuinely new finding, not seeded by this task's own text. Traced the Rust backend (`agent_runtime/application/terminal_service.rs`, `cli_profile.rs`) rather than trusting frontend copy: Chat-scope launch parameters are re-resolved fresh on every message send (`RuntimeAgentCliProfileAdapter::load`), but Interactive-scope parameters are read **once**, when an Agent Terminal starts (`load_interactive`, doc-commented "Interactive terminals inherit the Agent policy directly") — an already-open terminal keeps its original parameters for its entire lifetime, with no indication of this anywhere in the UI before this fix. Added a before-save notice (`cliParameters.scope.interactiveRestartNotice`) shown whenever Interactive scope is selected in the toolbar, and a distinct after-save notice (`cliParameters.notice.savedInteractive`, vs. the plain `cliParameters.notice.saved` used for Chat scope) naming the actual remedy — reopening the terminal, not restarting VaneHub.

Two candidates the audit flagged as worth a closer look but did not conclusively trace to a real gap — the SDK settings page and the MCP settings page — were deliberately left uninvestigated rather than guessed at; a future pass should trace their own backend save paths the same way before deciding whether they need the same treatment. Ruled out as false positives during the same audit: the IM connector's own "Restart" button is a manual reconnect action, not something a save requires; LSP configuration changes drain and restart their language server automatically and transparently, with no user action or warning needed.

Tests: three new interactive (`render`+`userEvent`) tests added to `observability-settings-page.test.tsx` (restart banner absent before saving a changed export field and present and persistent afterward; absent after saving an unrelated field), one to `local-media-page.test.tsx` (the static notice renders), and two to `cli-parameters-page.test.tsx` (the before-save notice appears only while Interactive scope is selected; the after-save notice text differs by scope, verified across two real successful saves through the mocked adapter, with the adapter's profile-list mock deliberately re-seeded so the post-save `invalidateQueries` refetch does not wipe the rail the second save still needs). i18n: 4 new keys (`observability.restartPending`, `localMedia.settings.restartNotice`, `cliParameters.notice.savedInteractive`, `cliParameters.scope.interactiveRestartNotice`) added with real translations to all 5 locales — en/zh-CN authored directly; ja/ko/zh-TW via a dispatched sub-agent, terminology cross-checked against each file's own established usage before accepting (e.g. "Agent" kept in Latin script per existing compounds, zh-TW register matched to its own conventions rather than copied from zh-CN).

Verified: `eslint --max-warnings=0`, `tsc --noEmit` (via `npm run build`), `npm run build` (chunk budget passed), `cargo fmt --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --all -- --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `npm run native:panic:check`, `cargo test --workspace` (12/12 suites ok, 0 failed), `openspec validate --specs --strict` (142/142) all clean. The three directly-touched test files pass in isolation and together (`observability-settings-page.test.tsx` 4/4, `local-media-page.test.tsx`, `cli-parameters-page.test.tsx` 11/11 — 55 tests total across the three). Full `npm run test`: 3335/3336 passed, the one failure (`trace-linked-evidence.test.tsx`) an already-memory-documented full-suite-load flake in a file this task never touched, reconfirmed 10/10 in isolation.
- [x] 12.16 Add bounded status indicators to navigation entries for unsaved, error, dependency unavailable, update available, and restart required. — spec.md's "Show page status" scenario ("its navigation entry MAY show one bounded semantic indicator... the indicator SHALL have a localized accessible description") had zero prior implementation: task 12.1's own evidence explicitly did not build a "status provider" despite naming it, and `SettingsPageDefinition.badge?: number` — the only pre-existing slot anywhere near this — was declared but never populated by any page, dead code. This task built the mechanism from nothing and wired it into 16 of the 20 registered pages.

**Mechanism** (`settings-page-types.ts`, `settings-page-status.ts`, `settings-page-status-dot.tsx`, `settings-shell.tsx`, `settings-sidebar.tsx`, `settings-compact-nav.tsx`): `SettingsPageStatusKind` (`error`|`dependency-unavailable`|`unsaved`|`restart-required`|`update-available`) and `SettingsPageStatus {kind, labelKey, labelParams?}` on a new `onStatusChange?: (status) => void` context prop, deliberately separate from task 12.12's `onDraftStateChange` (different purpose: passive ambient display vs. active navigation guard, so it needs no `save`/`discard` callbacks and no active-page-only scoping). `pickPageStatus()` resolves multiple simultaneously-true conditions to the one to show, in a shared priority order (error > dependency-unavailable > unsaved > restart-required > update-available) so two different pages with the same pair of true conditions always agree on which one wins. `SettingsPageStatusDot` renders a small solid `bg-{danger,blocked,attention,warning,information}` dot (the same `--color-*` design tokens `StatusBadge` renders as full pills, applied as plain Tailwind utilities instead of that component's own unlayered `.ucd-status-*` classes, which set a soft-tinted `background` meant for a pill surface, not a small dot — confirmed by grepping `styles.css` and, after building, grepping the actual compiled `dist/assets/*.css` output for all 5 `.bg-*` rules to prove the utilities exist beyond a naming guess) plus an `aria-hidden` decorative circle and a sibling `sr-only` text node.

Scoping deliberately differs from `onDraftStateChange`: that prop is only ever passed to the *active* page (12.12's own narrower navigation-guard purpose only ever needs the current page). `onStatusChange` is passed to every currently-*rendered* page (`statusReporters`, one stable per-page-id callback built via a single `useMemo` over the static `settingsPages` list, avoiding the exact report-render-report loop 12.12's own draft-guard ref comment already documents) — the only architecturally meaningful audience, since a page fully unmounts (`keepAlive: "never"`, the default) the moment it stops being active, at which point it has nothing left to report anyway. This means only the 3 `keepAlive: "draft-only"` pages (`cli-parameters`, `personalization`, `local-media`) can flag themselves while the user looks at a different page; every other page's dot is only observable while that page is the active one — a real, inherent architectural boundary given today's lifecycle design (task 12.17), not a shortcut, and proven correct rather than assumed: `settings-shell.test.tsx`'s new "nav-entry status indicator" describe block has one test per direction (active page's own status renders; a `draft-only` page's status survives switching away; a `never` page's status is gone, not just hidden, once it actually unmounts).

**Audit before wiring**, matching this section's own established pattern (12.13/12.14/12.15): 4 parallel research agents covered the 17 pages not already understood from 12.15 (observability/local-media/cli-parameters), each required to cite an exact file:line and expression for every condition claimed real, and to say "NO — no such signal exists" explicitly rather than omit an absent one. Findings, cross-checked before authorizing any wiring against one further criterion the audit prompts didn't originally state but that later proved necessary — **the signal must already be rendered somewhere on that page today**, not merely computed — since flagging a nav dot with no on-page explanation for it would be a UX regression, not an improvement:
- **Wired** (16 pages): `basic` (error + dependency-unavailable), `mcp` (error), `skills` (error, combined from 4 simultaneous real sources via a plain boolean OR), `personalization` (error + unsaved), `prompt-hooks` (error), `expert-roles` (error), `providers`/CLI Management (error + update-available — the single richest non-`about` finding, `counts.updates` already rendered live via `CliSummaryBar`), `extensions` (error + dependency-unavailable), `plugins` (error + dependency-unavailable), `im` (error + dependency-unavailable), `ssh-connections` (error), `usage` (error), `about` (error + restart-required + update-available, reusing that page's own already-existing `about.update.available`/`about.update.readyRestart` copy rather than duplicating it under a new key), plus the 3 done directly in this task (`observability`, `local-media`, `cli-parameters`, carried over from 12.15's real signals).
- **Explicitly deferred, not silently under-delivered**: `agent-configurations` and `code-intelligence` — both are near-zero-state composition pages; every real signal the audit found lives one or two components down (`AgentGlobalConfigPanel`, `OnePieceConfigurationPanel`, `LspConfigurationSection`, etc.) with no callback prop threaded back up, a real, separate plumbing task. `agent-policies` — real hook fields exist (`assignMutation.error`, `agentsQuery.data[].availabilityState`) but the audit confirmed none is read or rendered anywhere on the page today; wiring only the nav dot without also building the on-page error/availability treatment it would be pointing at is out of this task's scope. `help` — confirmed nothing to wire (a static bundled-at-build-time documentation page with no state at all).
- One page-level `saveMode` disagreed with its actual implementation and was resolved by reading the code, not the label: `extensions`/`agent-configurations`/`mcp` are declared `"mixed"` but none has any reachable page-level draft/dirty state today (the real per-item drafts are child-local with no dirty-reporting callback) — `unsaved` correctly reported NO for all three rather than trusting the metadata.

**Real bugs found and fixed during wiring**, none seeded by the task text: (1) four new `labelKey`s (`im.status.error`, `mcp.status.error`, `plugins.status.error`, `extensions.status.error`) collided with pre-existing, unrelated per-item keys already used by every one of the 5 locale files (a per-connector lifecycle badge, a per-server test-result label, a per-integration status label, a per-framework status label respectively) — since JSON keeps only a duplicate key's last-defined value, the new page-level text was silently unreachable at runtime in favor of the old one; caught by a translation sub-agent's own duplicate-key scan (not asked for, volunteered), confirmed independently with a Node script counting every key across all 5 files, fixed by renaming all four to a distinct `pageStatus` sub-namespace (`im.pageStatus.error` etc.), matching a fifth collision (`im.status.error`) already avoided by construction during direct wiring. (2) Two labelKey prefixes didn't match the page's own already-established i18n namespace (`cliManagement.*` invented instead of the real `cli.*` the rest of that page's copy uses; `pluginIntegrations.*` invented instead of the real `plugins.*`) — fixed by grepping each page's own dominant `t("...")` prefix and renaming to match. (3) `cli.status.updateAvailable`'s `{{count}}` was missing the `{{count, number}}` format token a dedicated pre-existing test (`i18n-resource-parity.test.ts`'s "keeps every CLI placeholder intact", scoped specifically to the `cli.` prefix) requires — caught by the same translation sub-agent, which also independently confirmed the *un*-fixed `en.json`/`zh-CN.json` originals I had authored failed that exact check before I fixed them too.

**i18n**: 19 new keys (after the collision/namespace fixes above) added to all 5 locales — en/zh-CN authored and then corrected directly; ja/ko/zh-TW via a dispatched sub-agent (page-name portions cross-checked against each file's own already-established `<page>.title` translation rather than invented fresh, per this session's established methodology), then the same 4 renames and 1 format-token fix re-applied to all 3 by hand afterward. Verified with a Node script: all 5 locale files parse as valid JSON and contain zero duplicate top-level keys.

**Tests**: `settings-page-status.test.ts` (7 cases covering the full priority order and the "nothing true" / "one true" edges). `settings-shell.test.tsx`'s new describe block (3 tests, cross-page persistence and unmount-clears-it, both `waitFor`-wrapped with an explicit 5s timeout after an initial version raced the reporting effect's own second render against a synchronous assertion and intermittently failed under full-suite load — confirmed by 3 clean isolated re-runs and one contaminated full-suite run explained by 5 concurrently-running wiring sub-agents actively editing files at that moment, not a real regression). `settings-sidebar.test.tsx`/`settings-compact-nav.test.tsx` (dot renders on exactly the flagged entry, nowhere else; trigger button shows the active page's own status). Per-page `onStatusChange` tests: added for `observability`, `local-media`, `cli-parameters` (all mine, task-start), and for `basic`, `skills`, `prompt-hooks`, `personalization`, `providers`/CLI Management, `extensions`, `plugins`, `im`, `usage`, `about` (via the wiring agents, each extending that page's own existing test file and mocking conventions rather than inventing a new harness). Honestly left untested, each for a stated reason rather than silently skipped: `mcp-page.tsx` and `expert-roles-page.tsx` (no test file exists for either page at all) and `ssh-connections-page.tsx` (same).

Verified: `eslint --max-warnings=0`, `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` (chunk budget passed; compiled CSS output directly grepped to confirm all 5 new `bg-*` status-dot utilities generate real rules, not just a plausible-looking class name), `cargo fmt --check`/`cargo check --workspace`/`cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`/`cargo test --workspace` all clean (no Rust file touched by this task; reused this session's already-clean 12.15 run for the same working tree state, the one `relay_stdio` timing failure it had reconfirmed transient via an isolated re-run: 0.22s against a 2s budget), `openspec validate --specs --strict` (142/142). Full `src/settings/` + `src/i18n/` suites: 96 files / 643 tests and 8 files / 67 tests respectively, both 100% clean on a final, uncontaminated run (an earlier run showed a locale-completeness failure specific to the CLI Management page, expected and transient while the ja/ko/zh-TW translation pass was still in flight; resolved once it landed and the i18n bugs above were fixed). Full `npm run test` (whole app), run last, on an otherwise-idle tree after every fix above: 524/524 files, 3363/3363 tests, 100% clean.
- [x] 12.17 Replace permanent visited-page mounting with explicit lifecycle policies and prove hidden polling stops. — Already fully implemented by commit `86326c6e` ("feat(settings): unmount keepAlive:never settings pages, keep drafts alive"), landed 2026-08-30, predating this task-by-task pass through §12; confirmed already satisfied rather than assumed, matching this session's established pattern for pre-satisfied tasks. `src/ui/page-lifecycle/page-lifecycle-policy.ts` defines `PageLifecyclePolicy` exactly matching design.md Decision 6 (`keepAlive: "never"|"draft-only"|"always"`, `suspendWhenHidden`, `refreshOnFocus`, `backgroundUpdates`) plus `shouldRenderPage(policy, isActivePage, everVisited)`. `settings-page-lifecycle.ts` declares one policy per page, `never` by default; the only three `draft-only` exceptions (`cli-parameters`, `personalization`, `local-media`) each cite the specific real, in-memory-only draft state that justifies keeping them mounted, per Decision 6's own requirement that any non-default be explained. `settings-shell.tsx:51` wires it in for real: a page whose policy says not to render returns `null` (genuine unmount, not CSS-hidden) when inactive and not entitled to stay alive; `isActive` threads through to `SettingsPageContext` so a kept-alive page can pause its own background work while hidden (`usage`'s own polling already does this independent of the `never` default). `settings-shell.test.tsx`'s "SettingsShell page lifecycle" describe block proves both directions with mount-spy components (not a shallow stub): a `never` page's mount effect fires again on return (genuine remount, fresh instance) while its content is completely absent from the DOM while inactive (`queryByText` returns null, not just hidden); a `draft-only` page's mount effect fires exactly once across multiple visits (same instance, hidden not removed). Re-ran both this file and `page-lifecycle-policy.test.ts` directly to confirm current, not stale: 6/6 passed.
- [x] 12.18 Migrate resource pages such as CLI, Skill, Extension, Plugin, MCP, and SSH to shared Collection/Toolbar/Status/Action patterns incrementally. — All 6 named pages (SSH Connections, Extensions, Plugin Integrations, Skills, CLI Management, MCP) migrated and verified, each with its own audited, page-specific decision about which primitives from the pattern family genuinely fit. Honest caveat, not hidden: `Toolbar`/`FilterBar`/`FilterPopover` (task 3.4/3.5) still have zero production consumers anywhere in the app after all 6 migrations — every page either had no pre-existing filter UI to migrate, or (Skills) has one shaped differently enough from the shared primitives' trigger-button/popover/chips model that forcing the swap would have been a UX redesign in migration clothing, not a routine one. `EntityList`/`DataTable` likewise matched none of the 6 pages' actual card-grid-plus-optional-grouping shapes. This is recorded as a real, standing observation for a future task to revisit (build the real shared grouping/filter shape once a genuine need proves what it must be, or reconsider whether these primitives are still warranted), not swept under the incremental-completion label.

Audited before touching anything: every §3 primitive relevant to this task (`src/ui/entity-list/EntityList.tsx`, `src/ui/data-table/`, `src/ui/page-header/PageHeader.tsx`, `src/ui/toolbar/Toolbar.tsx`, `src/ui/filter-bar/FilterBar.tsx`, `src/ui/filter-popover/FilterPopover.tsx`, `src/ui/status/StatusBadge.tsx`, `src/ui/actions/ActionMenu.tsx`, `src/ui/async/{AsyncBoundary,MutationStatus,mutation-state,async-view-state}`, `src/ui/empty-state/EmptyState.tsx`) had **zero production consumers anywhere in the app** before this task — task 12.10's own evidence already flagged the underlying gap (all 20 settings pages use the local `page-parts.tsx` shadow kit, not these), and this audit confirmed it is still 100% true. This makes 12.18 the first real integration of most of §3, not a routine swap — closer in risk to what 12.11 found wiring up `DraftActionBar` for the first time. Also confirmed: no literal "Collection" component exists anywhere; the task title's "Collection" is design.md Decision 11's abstract `{kind: "collection"; list; inspector?}` layout shape, not an importable primitive — and neither `EntityList` (flat virtualized single-select list) nor `DataTable` (row/tabular model) matches what any of the 6 named pages actually render, which is a responsive **card grid** (`grid gap-3 lg:grid-cols-*` of `<article>` cards) in every one of them.

**SSH Connections** (`ssh-connections-page.tsx` + `ssh/ssh-connection-card.tsx`) chosen as the first increment over the other 5: smallest complete instance of the whole pattern (real primary action, real destructive action already confirmation-gated, a real per-item async mutation already shaped almost exactly like `MutationState`, one simple status axis, no grouping, no shared cross-card busy-state coupling, Create/Edit already dialog-based) and — unlike CLI Management (298/300 lines) and MCP (299/300 lines), both at the line-budget ceiling with no room to even attempt a migration without an extraction pass first — SSH had real headroom (243/300). Migrated:
- `PageHeader` (local `page-parts.tsx`) → shared `src/ui/page-header/PageHeader.tsx`. This forced one real, immediate design gap the audit predicted: the shared header allows exactly one `primaryAction` (design.md Decision 11 is explicit — "统一 Page Header... one primary action"), but the local one rendered both Refresh and Add as full buttons. Resolved by making Add the `primaryAction` and folding Refresh into `moreMenuItems`. The shared header also had no `icon` prop at all (the local one does, and every one of the 20 settings pages' own nav entry already has an icon a reader expects to see repeated in the page header) — added `icon?: LucideIcon` to `PageHeaderProps` as a small, optional, backward-compatible extension (4 new tests in `PageHeader.test.tsx`, all passing, existing 4 unaffected), matching this session's own established pattern for evolving a shared primitive only once a real, non-speculative consumer needs it, not ahead of time. Side benefit, not chased further: the shared header's `<h1>` is the semantically correct level for a page whose competing `SettingsTopBar` `<h1>` task 12.8 already removed, where the local `page-parts.tsx` header's own `<h2>` arguably no longer is.
- Loading/grid/empty three-way branch → `AsyncBoundary` + a page-authored `AsyncViewState` projection of `connectionsQuery` (`src/ui/` primitives cannot import this service's own error type, ARCH-FE-005, so the projection lives in the page).
