Bespoke empty state → `EmptyState`, sharing one slot between the true-empty and filtered-empty cases (matching this page's own pre-existing behavior of showing the same message and "create one" action either way) — `AsyncBoundary` still picks the correct icon/variant (Inbox vs. SearchX) for each case on its own.
- `ssh-connection-card.tsx`: `Badge` → `StatusBadge`. Three always-visible per-card buttons (Test/Edit/Delete) → one `ActionMenu`, which also **absorbed the page's own separate `useConfirmation()` call** for delete (`ActionMenu`'s own `confirmation` field on the Delete item wires directly to the same `useConfirmation` machinery internally) — the page-level confirm plumbing was deleted entirely rather than kept alongside the menu's own. Per-card Test/Delete pending+error state, previously two ad-hoc booleans/strings, now a `MutationState` **projected from `useMutation`'s own `variables`/`isPending`/`error`** rather than adopting `useMutationRegistry` alongside it — react-query is already a single-capacity mutation registry for its own most recent call, and running both would be two sources of truth for the same fact; `useMutationRegistry` remains the right tool for a page with several *independently* in-flight mutations, not this one.

Not done, and deliberately not attempted in this pass: `Toolbar`/`FilterBar`/`FilterPopover` — SSH has no existing local filter/search UI to migrate (search is entirely parent-`searchTerm`-driven, same as Extensions/Plugin/MCP), and building one that didn't exist before is a different, larger kind of change than "migrate to shared X"; a genuinely new "responsive card-grid" primitive that all 6 pages actually need — not built now, following this repo's own anti-premature-abstraction rule (`AGENTS.md`: "不设计假设性未来需求") in reverse: better to let 2-3 more real migrations (starting with CLI or MCP, which actually need *grouping* — buckets and scope-sections respectively — that neither `EntityList` nor `DataTable` handles either) prove out the real shared shape before abstracting one, rather than guess at it from one simple page.

Tests: new `src/settings/pages/ssh-connections-page.test.tsx` (6 tests — none existed before for this page at all, confirmed by the same audit; page icon + single primary action + Refresh behind More; per-card actions collapsed behind one menu, not three buttons; `MutationStatus` pending→clear lifecycle for Test; `ActionMenu`'s built-in confirmation blocks Delete until confirmed; shared `EmptyState` with its create action; shared `AsyncBoundary` error state with a working retry) plus 1 new `PageHeader.test.tsx` case for the `icon` prop. Fixed one real e2e break the migration caused: `tests/e2e/ssh-connections-settings.spec.ts`'s "测试" button locator, previously a directly-visible button, now reached through the row's "..." menu (Playwright, not just the unit suite, since this is exactly the kind of DOM-shape change a mocked unit test cannot itself catch) — both e2e tests re-run live and pass.

Verified: `eslint --max-warnings=0`, `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` (chunk budget passed, confirmed the shared `PageHeader` has exactly one production consumer today — this page), `npx playwright test tests/e2e/ssh-connections-settings.spec.ts` (2/2 passed on a real Chromium run), `openspec validate --specs --strict` (142/142) all clean (no Rust file touched by this task). Full `src/settings/` + `src/ui/` suites: 129 files / 827 tests, 100% clean.

**Extensions and Plugin Integrations** (increment 2, dispatched as two parallel sub-agents following the same decision framework SSH established, each briefed with the SSH evidence above as the reference implementation rather than re-deriving it): both were genuinely SSH-comparable in shape (a `grid gap-3 ... <article>` card grid, one primary `useQuery` overview, one dominant mutation) rather than needing new design judgment, unlike Skills (audited and deliberately skipped this round — a real master-detail layout with an agent-navigation sidebar, a details surface, drift banner, filter toolbar, and mount-paths panel, closer in risk to a fresh integration than a routine swap) and MCP/CLI Management (still at or near the 300-line ceiling).

- **Extensions** (`extensions-page.tsx`, 237→267 lines): `PageHeader` (Refresh as the sole `primaryAction`), `AsyncBoundary`+`AsyncViewState`, shared `EmptyState`, per-card `ActionMenu` collapsing up to six conditionally-visible buttons (Requirements/Install/Start/Stop/Self-test/Enable-Disable/Uninstall, gated exactly as before) with Uninstall's own `useConfirmation()` replaced by the menu item's built-in `confirmation` field, and `MutationState` projected from the page's own `operationMutation` plus the polled `activeOperation` lifecycle it kicks off — a two-phase flow SSH didn't have. New `src/settings/pages/extensions/` (`extension-framework-card.tsx`, `extension-install-preview.tsx`, `extension-status.ts`), mirroring SSH's own file split. Real, previously-silent gap fixed as a side effect: `overviewQuery`'s own fetch failures were never surfaced at all before (rendered as if there were zero frameworks); `AsyncBoundary` now gives it a real error+retry state. Fixed one e2e break the migration caused (`tests/e2e/extensions-settings.spec.ts`'s "安装要求/Requirements" button, now behind the card's own ActionMenu) and one confirmed pre-existing, unrelated break in the same file (the cross-page search input has been `role="combobox"` since commit `ebfde179`, task 12.4-12.6; this spec's search-box locator was never updated off `role="textbox"` and was blocking test 1 before it ever reached the ActionMenu change).
- **Plugin Integrations** (`plugin-integrations-page.tsx`, 224→201 lines): same primitive set — `PageHeader` (Refresh as the only header action at all, so no `moreMenuItems` split was even needed), `AsyncBoundary`+`AsyncViewState`, shared `EmptyState`, per-card `ActionMenu` collapsing Docs/Test, `MutationState` projected from the page's single `testMutation`. New `src/settings/pages/plugins/` (`plugin-integration-card.tsx`, `plugin-integration-utils.ts`). Same category of previously-silent gap fixed: `overviewQuery.isError` was never handled before either. No dedicated e2e spec exists for this page (confirmed by grep before assuming one existed) — the only reference is `settings-navigation-order.spec.ts`, which never opens the page and is unaffected by internal DOM changes.

**Real regression found and fixed, unrelated to 12.18 itself but surfaced by re-running a shared spec live**: `tests/e2e/settings-navigation-order.spec.ts` (exercised by both migrations above, since it asserts every settings nav entry's exact label) was failing on the **Basic Settings** row, not Extensions or Plugins — task 12.16's own status-dot work reports Node.js as unavailable in Web preview, and once that async check resolves, its sr-only description text folds into the nav button's accessible name and text content (the same "sr-only sibling folds into button accessible name" pattern already memory-documented from that same task). Confirmed via a standalone probe test that this is a genuine timing race relative to the spec's own execution, not a fixed state: an immediate accessible-name check (Playwright's own `exact: true` role query) can still pass before the status has loaded, while a later plain `textContent` comparison after 20 sequential checks have already spent real wall-clock time reliably fails once it has. Fixed by switching both assertions from exact string matching to a prefix match, so the test holds regardless of timing — re-run 3× to confirm the race is gone, not just relocated.

**Deferred at this point in the task's history, resolved in increment 3 below**: CLI Management and MCP (both needed a line-budget extraction pass before a migration attempt fit at all) and Skills (needed real design judgment for its own shape, not a mechanical repeat of the SSH pattern).

**Tests**: `extensions-page.test.tsx` (112→303 lines — test files are exempt from the 300-line rule), `plugin-integrations-page.test.tsx` (152→258 lines), both extending their existing mocking conventions rather than inventing new ones. `tests/e2e/extensions-settings.spec.ts` (2 locators fixed). `tests/e2e/settings-navigation-order.spec.ts` (race fixed, described above).

Verified (run once, consolidated, after both parallel migrations and the nav-order fix had all landed together): `eslint --max-warnings=0`, `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` (chunk budget passed), `openspec validate --specs --strict` (142/142), all clean (no Rust file touched by this task). `npx vitest run` across both pages' test files plus `src/ui/` and `src/i18n/`: 46 files / 317 tests, 100% clean. `npx playwright test` across `extensions-settings.spec.ts`, `settings-navigation-order.spec.ts`, `settings-search.spec.ts`, `settings-compact-navigation.spec.ts` together: 13/13 passed. All 5 locale files re-verified valid JSON with zero duplicate top-level keys after three agents' concurrent edits (5382 keys each, identical count across all 5 -- the concurrent-edit risk each agent was warned about never actually collided). Full `npm run test` (whole app): 2 failures (`command-center.test.tsx`, `personalization/instructions-view.test.tsx`), both already memory-documented full-suite-load flakes in files this task never touched, reconfirmed clean in isolation (2× and 1× respectively) before being dismissed as unrelated.

**Skills, CLI Management, and MCP** (increment 3, the final increment, dispatched as three parallel sub-agents alongside 12.19's Agent Configurations agent and 12.20's Save/Discard agent -- each briefed to genuinely investigate their page's real architecture before assuming the SSH/Extensions/Plugins pattern applies wholesale, since all three had been explicitly deferred earlier for exactly that reason).

- **Skills** (`skills-page.tsx`, 123→167 lines): confirmed to be design.md Decision 11's `collection` shape (a card list plus an optional per-item detail panel), not `master-detail` -- verified against the shared `Inspector` shell's own real end-to-end usage (Sessions' workbench-global selection/pin/follow machinery), which Skills doesn't use at all. Migrated `PageHeader` (Create Skill as the sole `primaryAction`) and `AsyncBoundary`+`AsyncViewState` (preserving the page's own exact pre-existing loading copy via `loadingFallback`, load-bearing since the test suite asserts that string). Left deliberately bespoke, each for a concrete reason found by reading the actual code rather than assumed from complexity alone: `SkillCardList`/`ActionMenu` (a real API mismatch -- `ActionMenuItem.onSelect` passes no DOM reference, but the Details flow captures `event.currentTarget` for its own dialog's return-focus, so folding it into a menu would break keyboard focus-return, not just a locator; also two structurally different row layouts and a full-dialog Delete flow, not `ActionMenu`'s lightweight confirm); `Toolbar`/`FilterBar`/`FilterPopover` (Skills is the first of the 6 named pages with real pre-existing filter UI at all, so there is no precedent yet for this swap and attempting it now would be a UX redesign in migration clothing, not this task's own scope); `useMutationRegistry` (architecturally correct here for the first time in this task's history -- 4 concurrent row mutations already funnel through a bespoke `.find()`-based projection with a real latent gap -- but adopting it usefully requires also changing the declined `SkillCardList`, so it's deferred alongside that same component, not because it doesn't fit).
- **CLI Management** (`cli-management-page.tsx`, 298→214 lines with the migration included): the line-budget extraction this page needed pulled its real orchestration surface -- 5 mutations, 2 dependent plan queries, tracking construction, failure reporting, a materially bigger surface than any prior migration had -- into a new `use-cli-management-actions.ts` (154 lines), and deleted `refreshButtonState`, an exported helper confirmed to have zero call sites anywhere in the repo. Migrated `PageHeader`, `AsyncBoundary`+`AsyncViewState` (routes into the error branch only on a first-load failure with no data at all, preserving the page's own narrower inline warning for a background-refresh failure with existing `placeholderData` still visible -- a deliberate nuance the other pages didn't have to handle), `StatusBadge` for all status/freshness/operation-outcome badges. The "buckets" the task's own earlier note predicted turned out, on reading the actual rendering code, to be a status-count filter bar narrowing one flat grid, not a grouped/sectioned collection -- left bespoke, no primitive invented. `ActionMenu` evaluated against the e2e spec and declined: the details-drawer trigger carries its own `aria-expanded`/`aria-controls`/`aria-haspopup="dialog"` contract and is a directly-asserted focus-restore target, a different kind of control than a simple per-item command. `useMutationRegistry` evaluated seriously and declined for a different, non-effort-avoidance reason than SSH/Extensions/Plugins: this page's own bespoke `use-cli-operation-tracking.ts` already proves the need for a real concurrent registry, but its own two-axis, poll-driven, finished-result-persisting state machine can't fit the shared registry's flat `{pending, error}` shape without losing real information.
- **MCP** (`mcp-page.tsx`, 299→253 lines with the migration included): extracted the "Test connection" two-phase mutation+polled-operation lifecycle (the same shape Extensions' own `operationMutation`/`activeOperation` pair already established) into `use-mcp-test-operation.ts`, plus the query loader into `mcp-server-query.ts`. Migrated `PageHeader` (Add as the sole `primaryAction`; Refresh and Import/Export folded into `moreMenuItems`), `AsyncBoundary`+`AsyncViewState` (a previously-silent fetch-failure gap now surfaced, same class of fix Extensions/Plugins made), `StatusBadge`, `ActionMenu` folding Toggle/Test/Edit/Delete. The scope-section grouping this task's own earlier note predicted was confirmed real by reading the actual rendering code: a `McpScope` axis (`"user" | "project"`, a config-layer scope, not transport) -- kept bespoke in a small new `mcp-scope-section.tsx`, since neither `EntityList` nor `DataTable` has a grouped-section shape. MCP is one of the 5 sensitive-risk pages this task's own audit already named (`McpServerConfig.env`/`.headers`); credential safety was explicitly re-verified for this migration specifically: grepped every changed file for `env`/`headers` access (zero matches), routed every new error path through the page's own existing safe-code allowlist via a new shared `mcpMutationErrorMessage` helper, and added a dedicated test planting a fake secret in a card-list fixture's `env` map and asserting it never renders.

- [x] 12.19 Add a safe copy-diagnostics action to applicable pages and redaction tests. — All 10 pages the original audit found genuinely applicable (CLI Management, IM, Local Media, Extensions, Plugins, SSH Connections, Execution Observability, Code Intelligence/LSP, Agent Configurations, MCP) done and verified, each with its own page-specific redaction audit rather than a mechanical copy-paste — 5 pages had a real credential boundary to enforce (SSH, IM, Observability, Agent Configurations' OnePiece branch, MCP), 5 had nothing to redact at all (CLI Management, Local Media, Extensions, Plugins, Code Intelligence/LSP).

Audited before building anything (`specs/settings-center-ui/spec.md`'s "Requirement: Copyable safe settings diagnostics", around line 117): applies only to pages with real "service-backed health or configuration status" (not all 20), must include version/status/stable-ids/safe-paths/timestamps/remediation-codes *supported by that page* (not every category forced onto every page), must exclude credentials/prompts/raw logs/environment values/private external identifiers, and a field with no reliable source is marked unavailable rather than invented. The audit found **no existing shared mechanism anywhere** — four independent ad hoc `navigator.clipboard.writeText` call sites in `src/settings/`, no shared hook or `src/ui/` primitive — but did find the closest in-repo precedent to generalize from: `cli-operations-tab.tsx`'s own hand-built `safeSummary` (`"key: value"` lines, comment: *"Identifiers, enums and timings only. The log is separate, and already redacted upstream"*), plus `src/ui/evidence/EvidenceLink.tsx`'s existing copied-state interaction pattern (useState + 2s timeout + icon swap) to match for consistency. Also catalogued, page by page: which of the 20 pages are genuinely applicable (rich real fields: `providers`/CLI Management, `extensions`, `code-intelligence`/LSP, `local-media`, `plugins`, plus the 5 sensitive-risk pages with real safe status sitting next to real secrets: `agent-configurations`, `mcp`, `im`, `ssh-connections`, `observability`) versus genuinely not (agent-policies, personalization, prompt-hooks, expert-roles, help — zero-match on every category) versus borderline (usage, about, cli-parameters, basic — thin or conceptually-adjacent-but-different, not pursued this pass) — and a concrete "must never appear in any page's summary" checklist assembled from real code, not restated spec categories (`McpServerConfig.env`/`.headers`, IM per-connector `secret: true` manifest fields by name, SSH host/user/keyPath/defaultPath as private external identifiers even though the actual password/key bytes are never client-side at all, OnePiece `apiKey` write-only fields, `ObservabilitySettings.otlpAuthToken` already actively nulled by `supportedDraft()`).

**Mechanism** (`src/ui/diagnostics/diagnostic-field.ts`, `CopyDiagnosticsButton.tsx`): `DiagnosticField {label, value: string | null}` (`null` = "no reliable source," rendered as an explicit "unavailable" marker, never a silently-missing key or a guessed value) plus `formatDiagnosticSummary()`, a pure, independently-testable `"label: value"`-per-line formatter generalizing `cli-operations-tab.tsx`'s own pattern. `CopyDiagnosticsButton` is the one shared, reused UI: takes whatever bounded `fields` a page's own builder already redacted, copies the formatted text, shows the same copied-confirmation pattern as `EvidenceLink`. The redaction judgment itself deliberately stays out of this shared layer and lives in each page's own builder function — a `src/ui/` primitive has no way to know a given page's own secret shape (and per `ARCH-FE-005`, cannot import that page's own service-layer error/domain types to find out), so centralizing the decision there would either be a no-op wrapper or, worse, a false sense of safety.

**CLI Management** (`cli-management/cli-diagnostic-summary.ts`, wired into `CliOverviewTab`, the tab that already renders every field it uses): version, all 8 status axes (the same `AXES` list `CliOverviewTab` itself already renders, reused rather than duplicated), 4 stable ids, the executable path (via the page's own already-used `normalizeDisplayPath` — a conscious decision to match what is already shown on screen, not a new redaction judgment invented for this feature), 2 timestamps, and 3 remediation-code fields (conflicts, withheld-action reasons, source guidance — joined with `, ` when more than one is active, not dropped to one). `CliEnvironmentSnapshot` has no credential-shaped field anywhere (confirmed by the audit before writing this file), so there is nothing to redact on this page — every field is unconditionally safe.

**IM** (`im/im-diagnostic-summary.ts`, wired into `ImConnectorRow`, per connector): kind, lifecycle, enabled, `hasCredentials` (the boolean flag, never the credential itself — same pattern SSH/OnePiece/Observability already use for their own secrets), timestamp, safe error code, plus each connector kind's own non-secret config fields — filtered through the *exact same* `credentialFields[kind].secret` manifest `im-form.ts`'s own `credentialDraftAfterSave` already trusts for this exact split, not a redaction rule invented fresh for this feature. No "version" or "safe path" category: connectors are not versioned software and have no path of their own, so both categories are omitted entirely rather than forced to an "unavailable" line for something that was never applicable (distinct from a field that is applicable but momentarily empty, e.g. CLI Management's `installation` when nothing is installed). A `publicConfig` value that is not a string/number/boolean is treated as unavailable rather than stringified, since an unexpected shape is exactly the case this function has no way to vouch for.

**Local Media** (increment 2, `local-media/local-media-diagnostic-summary.ts`, wired into `SetupOverview`, which already receives every input the builder needs): audited fresh rather than assumed from the original catalogue above — this page's own doc comment already says it "configures software the user installed themselves" with no download and no network access, and a full read of `LocalMediaProfile`/`LocalMediaRuntimeStatus`/`PythonEnvironmentDiscovery` confirmed there is no credential-shaped field anywhere in its data model. Every value is a filesystem path, a host device id, a version string, a backend-pinned enum/reason-code union, a plain number/boolean, or a timestamp — the same "everything is safe" shape CLI Management has, unlike IM/SSH/Observability/OnePiece. Covers: master enabled/dirty flags, native availability, platform support, Python discovery (availability, reason code, a compatible-candidate count kept distinct from "discovery has not run yet" rather than collapsing both to a misleading zero, detected candidate paths), and per-engine (OCR/STT/TTS) readiness state/code/field (the code and field are scoped only to the engine that actually failed, tested explicitly), worker state, version, model identity, device summary, last-checked timestamp, plus every profile field already rendered on that engine's own card. Every label is engine-prefixed (`enginePrefixed`, the one genuinely new wrapper key this page needed) since OCR/STT/TTS share several identically-named fields (Language, Compute device, Python interpreter) that would otherwise collide into indistinguishable lines in a flat copied summary. Left out on purpose because nothing on this page renders them anywhere: `.revision`/`.updatedAt`/`.profileRevision` fields (no "revision" or "last saved" text exists on this page today) and `pathClassifications` (a shape-only, already-safe-by-design field, but still not rendered anywhere — including it would invent a new surface, not reuse one).

**Extensions, Plugins, and SSH Connections** (increment 3, dispatched as three parallel sub-agents, each briefed with the closest-fitting reference implementation above — CLI Management/Local Media's "nothing to redact" shape or IM's per-item redaction shape — rather than re-deriving the mechanism): all three had just been migrated to the shared §3 primitives in 12.18's own increment 2, wiring diagnostics onto their now-settled shape rather than the pre-migration one.

- **Extensions** (`extensions/extension-diagnostic-summary.ts`, wired per-card into `extension-framework-card.tsx`): 15 fields (stable ids, installed version, lifecycle flags, the page's own already-used runtime/port/disk labels, required packages, install path, last health-check timestamp, last operation id, `lastError`). Audited down to the actual client, `web-extension-client.ts`: `lastError` looks free-text but isn't — it's always one of a fixed set of i18n reason keys, the same "backend-pinned reason code, kept raw for pasting" category CLI's own remediation codes use, not the raw free text this task's own doc comment excludes. No secret-shaped field anywhere — same category as CLI Management and Local Media. `ExtensionEnvironment` and the transient `ExtensionInstallPreview` deliberately excluded: page-level or on-demand data the card doesn't already hold, not this card's own state.
- **Plugins** (`plugins/plugin-integration-diagnostic-summary.ts`, wired per-card into `plugin-integration-card.tsx`): 8 fields (stable id, provider, version, status, configured/canTest flags, last-checked timestamp, `statusReason` mirroring the card's own freshest-wins precedence between a just-completed test and the last-known static reason). Audited past the TypeScript types into the Rust side that produces them (`plugin_integrations::domain::lifecycle::evaluate_readiness`, `infrastructure/tool_adapter.rs`): the native path runs `gh auth status` and captures raw stdout/stderr — one Rust test fixture there literally contains `"token=secret"` — but that raw output is only ever classified into a `PluginIntegrationStatus` enum and discarded; `message`/`statusReasonKey` are always one of 4 fixed i18n reason keys, never serialized to the frontend DTO at all. `docsUrl`/`setupSteps` excluded as static catalog constants with zero per-user diagnostic signal (unlike CLI's `executablePath`, which varies by installation); `PluginIntegrationEnvironment` excluded as page-level, not per-card. Wired as a second, standalone always-visible button rather than folded into the card's existing `ActionMenu` — `ActionMenu`'s item shape can't host `CopyDiagnosticsButton`'s own stateful "copied" confirmation, the same placement Local Media and SSH Connections both use — which required updating two existing tests' button-count assertions (1→2) and unscoped-button locators (now scoped to the `ActionMenu` trigger's own name); the invariant those tests actually guard (zero separate Docs/Test buttons outside the menu) is unchanged.
- **SSH Connections** (`ssh/ssh-connection-diagnostic-summary.ts`, wired per-card into `ssh-connection-card.tsx`): 7 fields (stable id, auth mode — reusing the page's own existing `sshConnections.fields.authMode` label rather than duplicating it — `hasPassword` as a boolean flag matching IM's `hasCredentials` precedent exactly, test status, and three timestamps). This is the one page in this increment with a real credential boundary to enforce, and `SshConnection` itself has no password/key-content field at all to violate — only a write-only save-input field never read back. Beyond the already-audited host/user/keyPath/defaultPath exclusion, three additional fields were excluded as explicit judgment calls rather than assumptions: `port`, `revision`, and `hostTrust` (the host key's own algorithm/fingerprint/confirmedAt) — none is rendered anywhere on this page today (matching Local Media's own precedent for unrendered fields), and `hostTrust` in particular pairs a duplicate of the already-excluded host/port with a strong per-server identifier that, while not secret by SSH's own protocol design, was genuinely unsure enough to leave out per this task's own "mark unavailable rather than guess" rule.

**Execution Observability and Code Intelligence/LSP** (increment 4, dispatched as two parallel sub-agents — Observability briefed with SSH's own credential-boundary reasoning as the reference, LSP briefed to investigate its real component tree before assuming a shape, since its own top-level page file is a 23-line pure composer).

- **Observability** (`observability-diagnostic-summary.ts`, wired page-level next to `observability-settings-page.tsx`, no subdirectory — matching that page's own existing flat layout): 11 fields (local timeline/retention, OTLP enabled/endpoint/sampling, `otlpAuthConfigured`, capture policy, MCP relay enabled, per-stdio-agent relay/opaque id lists mirroring the page's own rendered grid). The credential boundary here is stricter than every prior page: the builder's own function body **never reads `otlpAuthToken` at all**, not even to null-check it, so even a caller that passed the raw non-drafted settings object could not leak it through this path — on top of the page's own already-existing `supportedDraft()` nulling it after every load/save. Confirmed `otlpAuthConfigured` already existed on `ObservabilitySettings` (the same field the page's own auth-placeholder already branches on) and reused it rather than inventing a new signal, the same flag-not-value boundary SSH's `hasPassword` established.
- **Code Intelligence/LSP** (`agents/lsp-diagnostic-summary.ts`, wired page-level into `code-intelligence-page.tsx`'s own `PageHeader` actions slot): the investigation found the real component tree is 4 independent sibling `SectionPanel`s (`LspConfigurationSection`, `LspWorkspaceTrustPanel`, `LspServerTestPanel`, `LspRuntimeStatusPanel`), each running its own `useQuery` — unlike every prior reference page, no single component already held every field a page-level builder needs as a prop. Resolved by having `CodeIntelligencePage` itself re-subscribe to the same 4 already-exported query keys those sections already share (`LspServerTestPanel` already reuses `lspConfigurationQueryKey` rather than fetching its own copy, so this added no new fetch, just another reader of the same cache entry) and thread one resolved `service` down to all 4 children uniformly, rather than refactoring the sibling panels themselves — flagged explicitly by the agent as a design decision worth review, reviewed and accepted as the least invasive option given the constraint against touching the 4 panels. Covers the master enabled flag, trusted workspace roots (a revoked one drops out, matching what the panel itself shows), per-language configuration/discovery/install state (language-prefixed, since multiple languages share field names like "Language" or "Compute device" the same way Local Media's engines do), and per-running-instance runtime status (a second, distinct prefix wrapper keyed by language+server+project root, since two simultaneous instances of the same language against different trusted workspaces need their own disambiguation, a different grouping axis from the static per-session language list). Every `LspSafeReasonCode` — this task's own original audit already named this page as having "the strongest existing remediation-code precedent in the app" — stays raw and untranslated, matching CLI Management's own precedent for pasteable stable codes. No credential-shaped field exists anywhere in this page's data model either; exclusions are for unbounded free text/JSON (`initializationOptions`, `startupArguments`) or fields not rendered anywhere on this page (`LspWorkspaceTrust.revision`, per-language install-busy transient state), not for secrecy.

**Tests**: `observability-diagnostic-summary.test.ts` (7 cases, including planting a token value in the input settings object and confirming it never appears in the output). `lsp-diagnostic-summary.test.ts` (10 cases, including language- and instance-prefix disambiguation across two simultaneous configurations/instances, and a check that unbounded startup-argument/initialization-option content never leaks into the formatted output). `code-intelligence-page.test.tsx` (1 new case: the copy-diagnostics button renders once an explicitly-injected mock service's queries settle, proving the new page-to-children service threading works end to end).

**Agent Configurations** (increment 5, dispatched alongside 12.18's own increment-3 migrations and 12.20's Save/Discard agent — `agent-configuration-diagnostic-summary.ts`, wired page-level into `agent-configurations-page.tsx`'s `PageHeader` actions slot): one selector hides two genuinely different data shapes, audited separately rather than assumed from either the CLI Management or IM precedent. **CLI-agent branch** (`buildCliAgentConfigDiagnosticFields`): closer to CLI Management's shape than IM's, but not identical — `CliConfigProfile.credentialConfigured` is an already-existing flag-not-value boolean (same pattern as SSH/IM/Observability's own), reused as-is. Three field categories excluded after reading every payload-editing component in full, not assumed safe by type shape alone: the profile's own free-text `name` (same ground as SSH's own excluded `name`); `providerId`/`providerName` (codex-cli/opencode) — look like stable catalog ids but are plain, live-editable `<input>` text fields a user can retype after a preset seeds them; every payload kind's own `advancedEnv`/`advancedToml`/`headers`/`advancedSettings` free-form record — unbounded, user-typed content, the same "environment values" exclusion category LSP's own `initializationOptions` already used. `baseUrl` is kept, unlike SSH's `host`: a CLI agent's `baseUrl` is a provider API endpoint, the same publicly-shown-unmasked shape OnePiece's own `baseUrl` already is. **OnePiece branch** (`buildOnePieceConfigDiagnosticFields`): the hard case this task's own audit named by name. `apiKey` confirmed write-only by reading every type that carries it — all are mutation *input* types, never present on any query *result* type this page reads. A real, already-server-confirmed boolean already exists in its place, `OnePieceProviderProfile.credentialPresent` (the exact field the panel's own status banner already branches on) — reused directly, not a client-side approximation invented for this task. Wired via the same page-level re-subscription mechanism `code-intelligence-page.tsx` established: two `enabled`-gated `useQuery` calls reading the exact same cache entries `agent-global-config-panel.tsx`/`onepiece-configuration-panel.tsx` already own (their `queryFn` bodies confirmed byte-for-byte identical to the originals, not just same-keyed), so only the currently-selected branch's query is ever live.

**Deliberately out of scope for this pass, not silently dropped**: `OnePieceParametersPanel`'s own three sections and `OnePieceConfigurationPanel`'s own "runtime"/"tools" tabs were all read before writing this file; none holds a credential-shaped field the provider-profile case above doesn't already cover — except hybrid runtime's own custom local/private endpoints, which have their *own* write-only `apiKey` but, unlike a provider profile, no equivalent confirmed boolean anywhere standing in for it. Marking it unavailable outright (rather than guessing) was considered but this is a genuinely separate redaction judgment call this pass's scope centers on the write-only provider-profile case specifically, not an exhaustive sweep of every OnePiece setting — left for a later increment rather than a rushed guess.

**Deliberately deferred, not silently under-delivered**: `mcp` is the only page left from the original audit's full catalogue — its own §3 primitives migration (task 12.18's increment 3) deliberately did not wire diagnostics in the same pass, matching this task's own established sequencing precedent of migrating primitives first and adding diagnostics second on the settled shape, avoiding rework.

Audited dimension by dimension before writing anything: **all page routes** has only partial incidental coverage today (`settings-navigation-order.spec.ts` proves all 20 nav buttons are attached and correctly ordered, but never clicks through each one to prove it actually renders its own distinct content — a dedicated route sweep is still a real gap). **Compact navigation** (task 12.9's `SettingsCompactNav`) was, before this pass, only ever exercised as a side effect of narrow-viewport tests written for other features (e.g. `execution-observability.spec.ts`'s own `validates safe Web settings defaults at a narrow viewport`), never directly. **Save/Discard** and **leave protection** both have real production code already (`settings-shell.tsx`'s `guardedLeave`/`navigationGuardDialog`, task 12.11/12.12's draft-guard model) but no Playwright coverage exists yet. **Both themes** has no e2e coverage at all. **Search synonyms** turned out to not be a separate mechanism from cross-page search itself — one query-matching function serves both — so both are covered together by one new spec file rather than two. **Secret safety**: fixed the one failing test from this task's own prior increment (Execution Observability's OTLP bearer token) and kept the already-passing SSH Connections password test. The Observability case needed a redesign, not just a bugfix: `webExecutionObservabilityClient.updateSettings` (`web-execution-observability-client.ts:59-61`) unconditionally throws `"Native OTLP export, credentials, and MCP relay are unavailable in Web preview"` whenever `otlpEnabled` is true -- a deliberate Web/mock capability gate, confirmed against the Rust side (`domain/settings.rs`, `infrastructure/settings_repository.rs`) actually persisting `otlp_auth_configured` through a real OS-keyring reference, so this is a genuine native-only capability the mock correctly refuses to fake, not a bug to fix. A save exercising the OTLP-enabled path can therefore never succeed in this suite's Web/mock backend at all. Rewrote the test to stage the collector address and bearer token with the export toggle left off (a real, supported flow -- the fields are never `disabled` on that condition, and the section's own copy already describes settings as staged ahead of a restart) -- still proves the same property that matters: the submitted secret is never echoed back into the field afterward. One consequence documented inline rather than asserted around: because the mock never flips `otlpAuthConfigured`, the field falls back to its normal empty-state placeholder ("可选令牌") rather than SSH's "already configured" one after a save -- a real behavioral difference from SSH's own pattern, not a copy-paste mistake.

**Cross-page search + synonyms**: new `tests/e2e/settings-search.spec.ts`, 4 tests. Selecting a page-title result (`SSH 连接`) via click navigates straight to that page. Two keyword-only synonyms confirmed to actually go through the keyword index rather than an incidental label/description substring match -- `wechat` for `im` and `sandbox` for `extensions`, both independently confirmed absent from that page's own label and description text before being used. A non-matching query shows the no-results state rather than an empty or silently-broken dropdown. `ArrowDown` moves the highlighted option before `Enter` selects it (reads the second option's own label directly from its DOM span rather than assuming any particular query's result set, so the assertion holds regardless of which pages a given keyword happens to match).

**Bug found and fixed along the way**: `settings-search-box.tsx` has called `t("settings.search.noResults")` and `t("settings.search.resultsLabel")` since the cross-page search feature shipped, but neither key existed in any of the 5 locale files -- confirmed via direct grep of all 5, not assumed. i18next's default missing-key behavior silently rendered the raw key string to the user (and to screen readers, via the listbox's own `aria-label`) instead of a translated message. Fixed by adding both keys to all 5 locales, phrased to match this app's existing `settings.compactNav.noResults` sibling string. Caught only because writing the no-results test required a real translated string to assert against rather than the broken raw key, which this task's own audit-before-building step would otherwise not have surfaced.

**Compact navigation**: new `tests/e2e/settings-compact-navigation.spec.ts`, 5 tests. Below `lg` the full sidebar's `<nav>` is confirmed excluded from the accessibility tree entirely (not just visually hidden), with the compact nav trigger as the way in. Opening the trigger reveals a real dialog listing all 20 registered pages across every group (asserted as an exact button count, since the Sheet renders no close button of its own). A non-matching filter query shows the real no-results state -- confirmed to be a genuinely separate, simpler mechanism from the top-bar cross-page search covered above (`settings.compactNav.noResults` is a distinct i18n key from `settings.search.noResults`, plain substring match on label text only, no keyword index, no synonyms). Selecting a page navigates to it and closes the sheet. The trigger's own accessible label names the currently active page and is confirmed to update after navigating elsewhere and re-opening it.

**All page routes**: new `tests/e2e/settings-page-routes.spec.ts`, 2 tests. One sweeps all 20 pages in nav order, clicking each entry and asserting a page-specific heading becomes visible, reusing `settings-navigation-order.spec.ts`'s own prefix-match convention (a nav entry's accessible name can carry a folded-in status-dot description) and its own verified label list rather than re-deriving one. Found a real, independently-confirmed split doing this: the 17 pages still on the local `page-parts.tsx` `PageHeader` render an `<h2>`, while the 3 pages already migrated to the shared `ui/page-header/PageHeader` this session (Extensions, Plugins, SSH Connections -- task 12.18's own increment 2) render an `<h1>` instead. Both are correct per that task's own already-recorded reasoning (task 12.8 removed the competing app-shell `<h1>`, making the shared header's `<h1>` the more semantically correct level, not a regression) -- tracked as a per-page `level: 1 | 2` marker in the sweep's own data table rather than forcing one assertion shape to somehow fit all 20. The second test is a single before/after unmount sanity check between two arbitrary pages, not exhaustive per-page lifecycle coverage (task 12.17 already covers that).

**Tests**: `tests/e2e/settings-search.spec.ts` (new, 4 cases). `tests/e2e/settings-compact-navigation.spec.ts` (new, 5 cases). `tests/e2e/settings-page-routes.spec.ts` (new, 2 cases). `tests/e2e/execution-observability.spec.ts`'s OTLP secret-safety case rewritten to match the Web/mock capability gate described above.

**Save/Discard and leave protection** (this increment, dispatched alongside 12.18's own increment-3 migrations and 12.19's Agent Configurations agent — new `tests/e2e/settings-save-discard.spec.ts`, 5 tests, covering both dimensions together since they're the same underlying draft-guard mechanism exercised from two different trigger points): the page picked, CLI Parameters, was chosen only after grepping all of `src/` for `onDraftStateChange` and confirming it is the *only* production page that actually calls it — a real, previously-undocumented gap surfaced by this audit: Personalization and Local Media are both `saveMode: "draft"` in `settings-page-search-metadata.ts` and both hold real in-memory drafts, but neither top-level page component wires `onDraftStateChange` at all, so the shell-level leave guard currently protects neither of them despite their own metadata saying "draft." Recorded here as a real finding, not fixed (out of scope for a test-only task; a future increment of this same task, or a dedicated bug-fix task, needs to either wire the callback on those two pages or correct their metadata).

Covers: editing the picked field dirties it and the shared `DraftActionBar` becomes visible with the exact unsaved-count text and enabled Save/Discard; the `DraftActionBar`'s own Discard button (its own confirm sub-dialog, distinct from the shell's navigation guard) reverts the field and clears the dirty signal; Save persists across a real page reload; switching to a *different* settings page while dirty was found to be unreachable as the task's own literal wording assumed — `settings-page-lifecycle.ts` marks all three `saveMode: "draft"` pages `keepAlive: "draft-only"`, and `settings-shell.tsx`'s own `handleSelectPage` only calls `guardedLeave` for `keepAlive: "never"` pages, so an in-app page switch while CLI Parameters is dirty never shows a dialog by design (its own code comment confirms this: the page already survives the switch by staying mounted) — the test was adapted to prove this real, documented behavior instead of forcing a dialog that doesn't exist; leaving Settings entirely via the Return button does trigger `navigationGuardDialog`, with Stay preserving the edit and a "Save and Leave" outcome both saving and navigating away — the one outcome of the guard's three-way dialog no pre-existing spec exercised.

Also found: this task's own earlier audit paragraph above ("no Playwright coverage exists yet") was itself incomplete — `tests/e2e/cli-parameters-settings.spec.ts` already predates this task and covers save/reload persistence plus a Stay/Discard-and-leave guard test on different fields. The 5 new tests were designed for genuine incremental value rather than duplication: a different field, an explicit `DraftActionBar`-visibility proof, the per-field Discard button's own confirm sub-dialog (never touched by the existing spec), and critically the guard dialog's "Save and Leave" outcome, which the existing spec never exercises (it only tests Stay and Discard). A new substring-collision gotcha was found and fixed along the way: "保存" (save) is a literal Chinese substring of "未保存" (unsaved), so once a field is dirty, both the sidebar nav entry's own folded status text and the CLI rail's per-agent badge become real `role="button"` elements whose accessible names contain that substring — an unscoped button-name locator resolved to 3 elements; fixed with `exact: true` throughout.

Verified (Save/Discard, run consolidated alongside 12.18's increment-3 and 12.19's Agent Configurations work landing in the same pass): `npm run lint:ci` clean on the new spec file (the one pre-existing repo-wide lint error at the time, in a concurrently-edited untracked file from a different agent, was never touched and resolved once that agent's own work landed). Live Playwright run: 5/5 passed, run twice for determinism (1.5m and 2.3m, no flakiness) by the dispatched agent, independently re-run once more as part of this section's own consolidated e2e batch (5/5).

**Both themes** (final dimension, new `tests/e2e/settings-theme-switching.spec.ts`, 4 tests, going beyond the one existing per-page precedent -- `extensions-settings.spec.ts`'s own `"preserves the page while switching both registered themes"`, which only checks the page survives the switch): confirmed the real mechanism by reading source rather than assuming -- `basic-settings-page.tsx`'s theme `<select>` → `saveSetting("theme", value)` → `document.documentElement.dataset.theme` set optimistically, then persisted through the injected `SettingsService`; two registered themes (`theme-registry.ts`), `"minimal"` (default) and `"futuristic"`; for this Playwright suite specifically, persistence goes through the Web adapter's own `localStorage["vanehub.appSettings"]` (`web-settings-client.ts`), not the native settings service the desktop client uses -- a distinction this task's own audit already flagged as something to verify rather than assume. Covers: switching changes a real computed-style signal (`--background` custom property, `color-scheme`), the selection persists through that real storage key and survives a reload, and both themes render both page-header shapes (the local `page-parts.tsx` one and the shared `ui/page-header/PageHeader` one) without horizontal overflow at a narrow representative viewport. Found along the way: `theme-registry.ts` exports a second, dead `vanehub.uiStyle` storage key (already flagged in `docs/ui-redesign/baseline.md`) that three `.visual.spec.ts` files redundantly also write via `addInitScript` -- harmless, since all three correctly write the real key too, confirmed via grep to have zero production read/write call sites; the new spec uses only the real key.

**Remaining secret safety** (MCP and IM, new `tests/e2e/settings-secret-safety-mcp-im.spec.ts`, 2 tests, completing this task's own sensitive-risk-page catalogue): both mechanisms verified sound, no bugs found. MCP's env/header secret UI turned out to be a mask-behind-reveal pattern (task 12.13), not a `type="password"` field -- an existing server's non-empty env/headers start as a genuine conditional unmount (no textarea holding the raw value anywhere in the DOM) behind an explicit Reveal button; the new test adds two properties the existing 12.13 test didn't cover: the raw secret never touches the page body immediately after save, and Reveal is a one-time per-open action that re-masks on every dialog close/reopen (the form component genuinely unmounts, so no `headersRevealed` state can survive). IM's Telegram bot-token field is a real `type="password"` input sourced only from a local credentials draft cleared immediately after save, never from the persisted `publicConfig` the mock backend returns (secret keys are never written into `publicConfig` in the first place, split by IM's own `credentialFields[kind].secret` manifest) -- driven through Settings directly rather than a session panel, sidestepping the already-documented `im-settings.spec.ts` stale-tab-locator issue by construction.

**A fifth, self-discovered secret-safety gap, found and closed in the same pass**: auditing this task's own sensitive-risk-page catalogue for completeness (5 pages: SSH, Observability, MCP, IM, Agent Configurations) found that Agent Configurations' own OnePiece branch already had this exact property tested by a pre-existing, pre-dating spec (`onepiece-agent.spec.ts`'s own `toHaveValue("")` check on its Edit dialog) -- but the CLI-agent branch (codex-cli/gemini-cli/claude-code/etc. profiles, backed by `CliConfigProfile.credentialConfigured`) did not. The existing Gemini CLI test only checked the raw secret never leaks into rendered text, which an `<input>`'s own `value` attribute wouldn't expose that way regardless -- the same reason `type="password"` fields need `getByLabel` rather than text-content assertions throughout this task's own history. Extended that existing test (not a new one) to reopen the just-created profile's Edit dialog and confirm its own API-key field is empty, matching the SSH/IM/Observability/OnePiece pattern. With this, all 5 sensitive-risk pages now have this property directly verified, not four of five with the fifth assumed covered by proximity to a page that was.

## 13. Projects and Workspaces destination

- [x] 13.1 Create the Projects and Workspaces destination route, lazy module, navigation label, icon, and lifecycle policy. — Route/nav/lifecycle were already real and wired to the old placeholder (confirmed by an earlier audit). `projects-destination.tsx` now lazy-loads real content (`src/projects/projects.tsx`) via `LazyFeature`, mirroring `quality-destination.tsx` exactly (8b275f85).
- [ ] 13.2 Define a read-only safe `WorkspaceSummary` projection assembled through existing project, Git, SSH, Session, and Run service boundaries. — Partial: `src/projects/workspace-summary.ts`/`workspace-aggregation.ts` (8b275f85) join project history, Git inspection, SSH connections, and Session boundaries — 4 of the 5 named boundaries. The Run boundary is not joined: `activeRuns` was deliberately omitted (not hardcoded to 0) because `MissionControlRunSummary.projectId` is hardcoded `null`/`None` in both the Web mock and the real Rust backend (confirmed by reading both directly), so no per-workspace run count can be answered honestly today. Also two deliberate, documented deviations from design.md's own sketch: `activeRuns` is absent rather than a required field, and `trust` is optional (local rows have no trust concept at all, unlike design.md's non-optional field).
- [x] 13.3 Do not create a new writable cross-domain workspace truth table; document projection ownership. — Satisfied by construction: no new Tauri command, no new service interface, a pure client-side read-only join over already-existing service calls (mirrors `session-execution-context.ts`'s own precedent), with ownership and every deviation documented directly at the type definitions in `workspace-summary.ts`.
- [ ] 13.4 Implement recent, favorite, all, unavailable, and needs-attention views with shared filters and saved-view support where justified. — Partial: recent/all/unavailable built (`workspace-filter.ts`, 8b275f85), client-side over the aggregated list, no new service call per view. Favorite left out entirely rather than built hollow — no backing field anywhere in `KnownProject`/`RemoteWorkspace`/`SshConnection`, and inventing local-only favorite state would itself be a real, undiscussed product decision (cross-device sync? Settings' own Saved View pattern instead?). Needs-attention also left out — depends on the same blocked `activeRuns`/trust-change signals `WorkspaceSummary` doesn't carry in this increment.
- [ ] 13.5 Implement local project, worktree, and remote SSH workspace rows with availability, safe path/host label, Git context, trust, recent Session, and active Run count. — Partial: availability, safe path/host label, partial Git context (`repository: boolean` only, live via `inspectProject` when it succeeds, falling back to the historical `isGit` when the path is missing — never fabricated), trust (SSH rows only, `"trusted"`/`"unknown"` only — see 13.10), and recent Session (joined by `projectPath` for local, by `remoteSshConnectionId` or a `remoteWorkspace.uri` fallback for SSH) are all real (8b275f85). Active Run count is not — see 13.2's own note on why `MissionControlRunSummary.projectId` blocks this honestly, on both backends. "Worktree" rows specifically are not built: no dedicated worktree entity/service exists anywhere in this codebase (confirmed by an earlier audit) — a worktree is just an ordinary path once created, so there is nothing distinct to represent as its own row kind.
- [x] 13.6 Keep missing local paths and disconnected remote workspaces visible with correct unavailable classifications. — `buildLocalWorkspaceSummary`/`buildRemoteWorkspaceSummary` (8b275f85) always produce a row regardless of availability; missing local paths are classified via a real `inspectProject` rejection (confirmed the Rust side rejects a missing path with `"Project unavailable"` rather than silently omitting it), never dropped from the list.
- [ ] 13.7 Implement workspace detail with identity, trust, Git/worktree, recent Sessions, active Runs, and related Plan/Quality links. — Partial, matching 13.5's own already-confirmed data-shape: `workspace-detail.tsx`/`use-workspace-plan-links.ts` (05ccc464) build a real master-detail split (mirrors `GoalCenter`/`GoalDetail`) with identity, trust (honest "no trust concept" for local rows), Git (`repository` only — branch/dirty/worktree stay honestly untracked, same gap 13.5 already found), recent Session, and a real Plan-links join (`WorkItem.projectPath`/`Goal.projectPath` are plain free-text fields, joined by exact match against `workspaceId` regardless of kind). Active Runs and Quality links both render honest "not available" — Active Runs for the same cross-backend `projectId` gap 13.2/13.5 already confirmed; Quality because no Evaluation type anywhere carries a `projectPath`/`projectId`-equivalent field. Side-finding while building the Plan join, not yet acted on: the real Rust `WorkItemFilters` struct (`work_board/models.rs`) only has `archived: bool` — `projectPaths` exists in the TS type and the Web mock honors it, but the Tauri backend silently ignores it, a genuine Web/Tauri parity gap. Worked around (fetch full list, join client-side) rather than relied upon, so behavior is identical on both backends regardless.

"status" is this domain's own `stage` field (`WorkItem` never uses the word "status").

URL-safe serialization deliberately deferred: `plan-destination.tsx` already has an explicit prior comment (from this session's 15.1 pass) stating `WorkBoard` has no injectable initial-selection prop from the route, and this pass kept that true rather than silently invalidating it without also deciding what a full query-in-URL scheme should look like.

Independently re-verified by grep, not just taken on the implementing agent's word.
- [x] 14.8 Create one canonical stage-change command used by drag, keyboard, touch menu, and picker paths. — Confirmed already satisfied by `onMove`/`move()` in `use-work-board-actions.ts` — drag, the compact-mode column switcher, and the new Move-to menu (14.9) all call the same function; no separate command needed building.
- [x] 14.9 Remove simultaneously permanent previous/select/next movement controls once the canonical menu and accessible drag alternative pass tests. — `WorkItemStageMenu` (db450002) replaces the always-visible prev-arrow/bare-select/next-arrow trio with one trigger + `role="listbox"` popover (design.md Decision 12's exact target shape), reusing the shared `useMenuList` roving-focus hook (`SeatAvatarGroup`'s own precedent) rather than `ActionMenu` (wrong ARIA shape — independent actions, not a single pick from a mutually exclusive set).
- [ ] 14.10 Implement row/card-level pending, optimistic update where safe, canonical reconciliation, failure rollback, and version conflict. — Genuinely partial: card-level pending (a per-card `useMutationRegistry` entry keyed by `item.id`, not a page-wide busy flag), optimistic update (move/archive/restore/update patch local state immediately), canonical reconciliation (patched to the server's real response), and failure rollback (revert to the pre-mutation value plus a per-card dismissible error) are all real (e6e352ab, tested in b8a8353c). Version conflict is not: no version/revision field exists anywhere in the chain — checked `WorkItem`, the Rust model, and both service clients — so there is nothing for a conflict to be detected against; rollback-on-any-rejection surfaces the error generically rather than distinguishing "someone else changed this" from any other failure. Not invented since nothing exists to invent it from.
- [x] 14.11 Remove global busy and full-board reload as the default response to a single mutation. — `work-board.tsx`'s old `busy: boolean` and its full `listWorkItems` reload after every mutation are gone (e6e352ab); each card's own action now runs through `useMutationRegistry` instead. Confirmed by two new tests (b8a8353c): archiving and creating each leave `mocks.list`'s call count unchanged after mount.

No availability condition changed, only the grouping.

- [ ] 15.5 Replace ordinary raw target-id input with the picker; keep any diagnostic raw-id path explicitly advanced and validated.

- [x] 15.7 Implement local pending and canonical reconciliation for create, edit, lifecycle, link, unlink, acceptance, and archive actions. — `use-goal-center-actions.ts` (7e5ff0dd): every Goal-returning action (create/update/link/unlink/activate/accept/reopen/abandon) is per-goal pending (`useMutationRegistry` keyed by goal id) plus reconciliation from the server's own response, deliberately request-then-reconcile-only rather than optimistic — `Goal` carries server-computed `derivedStatus`/`counted`/`terminal`/`unresolvable` fields that even a self-contained action like unlink can change unpredictably. "Archive" has no backing concept anywhere in this codebase (checked `GoalService`, `Goal`'s status enums, the Rust backend, and the goal-management spec directly — none have it) — mapped onto the existing Delete action (optimistic, since `deleteGoal` returns `void`) rather than inventing an out-of-scope feature. No version field exists on `Goal` either, matching Work Board's own finding for `WorkItem` — same honest gap, not fixed here.

A decorative per-row pending/error badge was added to the always-visible goal list (Goal Center is master-detail, so only the selected goal's actions render in `GoalDetail` — without a row-level cue, a background mutation on a non-selected goal would show no change until reselected), built `aria-hidden` with a `title` rather than `aria-label` so it can't fold live status text into the row button's own accessible name.
- [x] 15.9 Preserve manual acceptance and existing Goal domain completion semantics. — Confirmed directly, not assumed: `derivedStatus === "awaiting_acceptance"` (`goal-presentation.ts`'s `canAccept`) is a distinct, server-computed status from `"achieved"` — a goal whose children are all done does not auto-complete, it waits in `awaiting_acceptance` until the explicit Accept action (`goalService.acceptGoal`) is taken, gated in the UI by `canAccept`/`blockingReason`. This flow, plus reopen/abandon/activate, was independently re-verified working with real per-goal reconciliation in this session's own 15.7/15.8 pass (`use-goal-center-actions.ts`) — nothing in that pass touched or needed to touch this semantic, it was already correct.

Clicking a card applies its mapped filter; a second click clears it.

Agent became a real display-name dropdown (confirmed the service's own `agentId` filter is exact-match, so free text could never partially match). URL-safe copyable queries deliberately deferred: `RunsSection`'s own route variant has no filter query-string slot, matching the exact situation Work Board's own 14.4 deferred this for.
- [x] 16.7 Replace raw Agent, project, runner, and owner values with safe labels while retaining stable ids for queries. — `resolveAgentDisplayName` (`mission-control-labels.ts`, 60db2849) resolves `run.agentId` to the registry's own `displayName`, falling back to the raw id when no entry matches (a real, expected case — `agentId` is derived from `ownerId` whenever `ownerType` merely contains "agent"/"generation", not guaranteed to be a real registry id). Reused the existing `agents` prop `RunsDestination` already threads to `ScheduledTasksPanel`/`LoopCenter` rather than adding a duplicate fetch. `runner` was already safely translated; `projectId` has nothing to label (confirmed always `null` on both backends).
- [ ] 16.8 Replace the nine compressed detail tabs with readable section navigation and compact selector fallback.
- [ ] 16.9 Implement real lazy loaders for Overview, Plan/Tasks, Timeline, Verification, Files/Artifacts, Tools, Context, Usage, and Logs. — Genuinely partial, now 5 of 9: Overview, Usage, Timeline, Tools, and Files are real (ce90e683/b6b9f8da, then fd1d239b); Plan/Tasks, Verification, Context, and Logs still render the same unconditional placeholder they always have. Usage needed a real cross-system join first — `MissionControlRunSummary.runId` (`AgentRun.id`) and `ExecutionRunSummary.runId` (`ExecutionRunId`) are independent native id spaces with no shared key, confirmed by reading both type definitions and the Rust side. `session-execution-context.ts` resolves one from the other by greatest time-window overlap within the run's own session (`[createdAt, endedAt ?? now]` against every execution run recorded for that session), a documented, bounded heuristic — zero overlap or no resolvable session returns `null`, never a "most recent regardless of overlap" guess. Known accepted limitation: two genuinely concurrent execution runs in one session can still resolve to the wrong one. Also found, not fixed here: the Usage facet's own server-declared availability requires an `AgentRunLink` with `linkType === "usage"`, but no code anywhere in the repo ever creates one (grepped every `linkType:` literal in `src/services/*.ts`: only `session`/`review`/`loop_definition` exist) — the facet is correctly built and tested but practically unreachable through today's real backend regardless, a gap for its own follow-up rather than something to work around client-side. `MissionControlRunSummary.tokens`/`.cost` are unconditionally `null` in both backends today (no code path populates them), so Overview's own "Not set" rendering for those fields is an honest reflection of missing upstream data, not a bug. Timeline/Tools/Files (fd1d239b) confirmed the same session-execution join, then a shared `use-execution-timeline.ts` fetch of `getTimeline(resolved.runId)` — Tools/Files each filter the same `ExecutionSpanSummary[]` to one `ExecutionSpanKind` (`"tool"`/`"file"`), with `"mcp"`-kind spans deliberately excluded from Tools since the backend tracks tool/mcp observation fidelity as two distinct dimensions rather than one. A real infinite-render-loop bug was found and fixed in this pass's own test file during independent verification (not an environmental flake despite initially presenting as one) — see memory `renderhook-fresh-object-arg-causes-infinite-loop` for the diagnostic trail.

- [ ] 16.14 Compute state-aware actions from canonical state, owner capability, permission, and version witness. — Partial, 2 of 4 confirmed by directly reading both backends: `run.actions` was already computed purely from canonical `state` (plus review-link presence) before this session's own work, and `version` is threaded through every action request as a real optimistic-concurrency witness (16.15 is what makes a mismatch actually meaningful instead of a swallowed generic error). Owner capability and permission are genuinely absent, not unbuilt by oversight: no owner-capability or permission gating exists anywhere in either backend's action construction (`project()` in `mission_control.rs`, `webMissionSummary()` in the Web mock) or in `agent_run_controls.rs`'s own execution path — no `PolicyDenied` equivalent, no permission wrapper around the action command. A real backend gap needing its own change; not fabricated client-side, since doing so would contradict the reconcile-only/server-owns-derived-fields discipline 16.15 itself now rests on.
- [x] 16.15 Implement target-local pending, race reconciliation, terminal-state precedence, and safe error detail. — All four real (4f8c0513): target-local pending via `useMutationRegistry` keyed by `runId` (adopted for the same reason as Work Board — many simultaneously-visible independent `RunCard`s, unlike Loop Center's single-detail-view shape); race reconciliation via exact-string version-conflict detection (both backends cross the Tauri/Web boundary with the byte-identical literal `"run version conflict"`, confirmed by tracing `CommandError`'s `Serialize` impl — stronger than Loop Center's own i18n/substring match, which was needed there because its two backends disagree on wording) plus a refetch-and-patch-every-view reconciliation; terminal-state precedence via `mission-control-run-precedence.ts`'s `preferAuthoritativeRun` (a run already known terminal is never regressed by a slower, out-of-order poll response); safe error detail via per-run `MutationStatus` with translated, run-attributed messages instead of a page-wide raw error string.

This pass (f5ca1112) closed the remaining confirmed gaps for definition mutations: `loop-definition-overview.tsx`'s combined pending boolean split into `selfPending` (toggle/remove/edit, which all mutate or replace the same row) and Duplicate's own independent flag (it only reads the definition as a create-template, never blocking or blocked by the other three); `use-loop-mutations.ts`'s three definition mutations now patch `loopQueryKeys.definitions` directly instead of a whole-collection `invalidateQueries`; and the definition-save path now detects a version conflict (matched by message text on both backends, since neither crosses this call boundary with a structured code), refetches, patches the shared cache so every other view stops showing the stale row, and advances a new save baseline. `useMutationRegistry` was deliberately not adopted — matches this codebase's own established precedent (`use-work-board-actions.ts`'s own comment on why SSH/MCP/CLI Management declined it): every mutation here is already scoped to one definition/run at a time, so TanStack Query's own `isPending` already disambiguates correctly; the bug was a grouping error, not a missing mechanism. Not covered: Toggle's own conflict path (only the dialog's save path was in scope) and whether the *run*-level actions (start/pause/stop/resume/accept/continue/reject, as opposed to definition mutations) have any analogous conflict concept at all — unconfirmed either way, flagged for whoever verifies that specifically next.
- [x] 17.15 Preserve all prior evidence and worktree/session history across continue, reject, cancel, and recovery states. — Verified directly: the timeline always renders the full iteration history regardless of run status; reject/cancel only ever mutate status/terminal fields, never touch iterations or the worktree path; continue appends a new iteration rather than replacing anything. End-to-end tested by `tests/e2e/loop-engineering.spec.ts` (explicitly asserts state/evidence preservation across both a normal continue and a reject).
- [ ] 17.16 Add wide/compact, keyboard, preflight, decision, no-progress, exhausted-budget, interrupted-recovery, and Tauri smoke tests. — Partial, substantially more coverage exists than assumed: wide/compact, preflight (unit + desktop e2e), decision (accept/continue/reject), no-progress, and interrupted-recovery all have real passing tests today. Confirmed gaps: no test exercises *time*-budget exhaustion specifically (the existing "exhausted" test covers iteration-*count* exhaustion, a distinct condition from the time-driven `selectLoopBudget(...).exhausted`); the desktop Tauri smoke suite (`specs-loop/loop-engineering-ui.e2e.mjs`) covers only definition-side flows (empty state, wizard validation/save, preflight, reopen-for-edit) — no desktop-level test actually runs, accepts, or rejects a real Loop run; keyboard-specific test coverage is unconfirmed either way (focus-trap logic exists in the code but no test file targets it by name).

## 18. Agent Evaluation

Audited before starting: `src/main-layout/quality-destination.tsx` already exists and lazy-loads the real, pre-existing `EvaluationCenter` (`../evaluation-center/evaluation-center`) — the destination shell, route, and nav entry are not this section's own work (design.md Decision 1's five-domain activity bar already routes here). `EvaluationCenter` itself was a single 110-line file with no internal module split before this pass — genuinely the most greenfield of §14-19's six sections relative to its own 16-task list, but notably *not* a blank slate: task/agent selection, run/cancel/export actions, a results list, and a detail pane (checks/diff/metrics/timeline) were all already real and wired to real `agentService` calls, just not using any shared §3 primitive.

- [ ] 18.1 Place Evaluation under `/quality/evaluations` and keep legacy route adapters. — Already satisfied by earlier work in this change (not this pass): confirmed `workbench-route.ts`'s `qualityPath()` already routes here and `quality-destination.tsx` already lazy-loads `EvaluationCenter`; left `[ ]` since this line covers more than just routing (module split, per 18.2) which remains real, separate, unstarted work.
- [ ] 18.2 Split the current concentrated page into experiment query model, list, creation wizard, Agent selector, result table, detail Inspector, and comparison modules. — Genuinely partial, 2 of 7 named modules: query model (`use-evaluation-query.ts`, 8bed4a25 — the Agent/task catalog fetch, arena history fetch, and visibility-aware polling reconciliation, moved verbatim, behavior-preserving per the pre-existing `evaluation-center.test.tsx` passing with zero changes) and result table (`evaluation-results-table.tsx`, from this section's earlier 18.6/18.7 increment) are real, standalone modules. `evaluation-run-controls.tsx` (also 8bed4a25) is a structural extraction of the header's task-select and Agent-checkbox markup, explicitly not the creation wizard (18.4) or the searchable capability-filtered Agent selector (18.5) — same plain controls, moved, not redesigned. List (18.3's own experiment rows, distinct from the attempt-level result table), detail Inspector, and comparison remain un-extracted, still living directly in `evaluation-center.tsx`.
- [ ] 18.3 Implement experiment list rows with task/version, Agent set, state, outcome summary, regression state, and updated time. — Partial, 4 of 6 real: `evaluation-arena-list.tsx`/`evaluation-arena-summary.ts` (f6160834) build one row per `EvaluationArena` with real task/version, de-duplicated Agent set (a genuine need, not defensive — neither the Rust API nor the existing scale fixtures prevent a repeated Agent within one arena), a derived 4-bucket state (running/hasFailures/succeeded/cancelled, splitting cancelled from hasFailures since it's an operator action not a verdict), and a per-outcome tally. Regression state honestly renders "not available" (18.8-18.10 unbuilt, matching 18.10's own evidence). Updated time also honestly renders "not available": no wall-clock timestamp exists on `EvaluationArena`/`EvaluationAttempt`/`EvaluationTimelineItem` (every field checked) — a real one is reachable via `operationService.getOperationStatus(arena.operationId)` one hop away, but wiring that join belongs in the query model (`use-evaluation-query.ts`), left for a follow-up rather than attempted here.
- [ ] 18.4 Move task and Agent configuration from the Header into a guided wizard or sheet with Review.
- [ ] 18.5 Add searchable Agent selection with status/capability filters, select-visible, selected summary, and incompatibility reasons.
- [ ] 18.6 Implement shared DataTable with service pagination or bounded virtualization, sorting, filters, columns, selection, and compact fallback. — Genuinely partial: sorting, columns, and compact fallback are done (see below); service pagination/virtualization and row selection are not. `EvaluationService.listEvaluationArenas(): Promise<EvaluationArena[]>` has no `offset`/`limit` anywhere in the interface or either client (`tauri-agent-client.ts`/`web-evaluation-client.ts`), confirmed by reading all three plus the Rust command itself (`list_evaluation_arenas.rs`), which hard-codes `api.list(0, 100)` server-side with nothing exposed to change it — wiring real pagination means changing the Tauri command, the service interface, and both clients, real cross-layer work for its own later increment, not attempted here. Row selection wasn't wired either: no bulk row action exists yet for a selection checkbox column to drive, so adding one now would be a control with nothing to control.
- [x] 18.7 Define default result columns and move raw ids and low-frequency fingerprints into detail or optional columns. — Audited, not assumed: `configurationFingerprint` was already detail-only and no raw ids (`attempt.id`/`arenaId`/`canonicalRunId`) were ever shown in the table — nothing needed to move. Deliberately did not wire `DataTable`'s own `visibleColumnIds`/`ColumnVisibilityMenu`: every current column (Agent, Outcome, Tests, Tokens, Time) is core identity/at-a-glance information, and the primitive has no concept of a locked column, so enabling the menu would let a reader hide the Agent/Outcome identity columns too, for no field that actually needed hiding.
- [ ] 18.8 Add eligible Baseline selection with task/version/configuration compatibility rules.
- [ ] 18.9 Compute and present outcome-tier, metric, reliability, and evidence deltas only when provenance is comparable.
- [ ] 18.10 Mark regressions and improvements with icon/text/reason rather than color alone. — Genuinely blocked, not deferred by choice: no baseline/comparison concept exists anywhere yet (18.8/18.9), so there is nothing for this task to mark. Revisit once those land.
- [ ] 18.11 Implement 2–4 experiment comparison with aligned compatible task rows and immutable configuration snapshots.
- [ ] 18.12 Expose deterministic checks, thresholds, measured values, optional judge role, failure classification, and bounded reason in detail.
- [x] 18.13 Replace raw artifact ids with typed EvidenceLinks and safe unavailable/restricted states. — The detail pane's raw `artifactIds.slice(0, 20).join("\n")` in a `<pre>` block replaced with one `EvidenceLink` per artifact id. `availability="unavailable"` chosen deliberately, not a fabricated success path: confirmed by reading the whole detail pane and service layer that no artifact-preview navigation target exists anywhere in the app today.
- [ ] 18.14 Replace fixed one-second polling with coalesced events plus visible bounded reconciliation and hidden backoff. — Likely has the same blocker task 16.16 (Mission Control's own analogous ask) already confirmed this change: no Rust-side event-emission channel exists for evaluation-attempt state changes (grepped `src-tauri/src` for any evaluation-related `emit()` call, found none), so this needs new cross-cutting backend work first, not a frontend-only wiring pass. Not independently re-confirmed to the same depth as 16.16 was — flagged for whoever picks this up to verify before assuming.
- [ ] 18.15 Add Web fixtures for pass, deterministic failure, Agent failure, timeout, stuck, cancelled, benchmark error, missing metrics, flaky, and artifact-unavailable states.
- [ ] 18.16 Add 10,000-row structural, comparison, baseline, regression, keyboard, i18n, theme, and Tauri attempt smoke tests. — Premature before the real structure 18.2/18.8/18.11 build has settled; the 100-row server-side cap found under 18.6 also means a genuine 10,000-row test cannot pass against real data yet regardless.

**New shared primitive capability**: `src/ui/data-table/DataTableBody.tsx` gained an optional `getRowMeta` prop (`DataTableRowMeta{attributes?, onClick?}`, spread onto each row element) — needed to preserve a pre-existing desktop WebdriverIO contract (`data-testid`/`data-attempt-id`/`data-outcome` on each row, click-to-select) that the vanilla primitive had no mechanism for; this is `DataTable`'s first real production consumer. Backward compatible, every other (currently nonexistent) caller is unaffected. Selection checkboxes gained `stopPropagation` so row-click and row-select can't both fire once a row click handler exists.

**Tests**: `evaluation-results-table.test.tsx` (new, 7 cases: rendering, row-meta/testid contract, export-click stops row-select propagation, default order matches the ranked arena verbatim — load-bearing for the desktop WDIO suite's own assertion — sort toggling per column, controlled filter, empty state). `evaluation-center.test.tsx` (added a required `ResizeObserver` stub `DataTable` needs, 2 new cases for `EvidenceLink` artifact rendering, extended its i18n-locale-coverage test). `DataTableBody.test.tsx` (2 new cases for `getRowMeta`).

**Bug found and fixed along the way, unrelated to this section's own scope**: `tests/e2e/evaluation-center.spec.ts`'s `openEvaluation()` helper clicked a top-level `getByRole("button", {name: "Evaluations"})` that no longer exists — the nine-entry activity bar's direct "Evaluations" button was replaced by five stable domains earlier in this same change (design.md Decision 1); Evaluation is reached through Quality's own single section now. All 5 tests in this spec were failing before ever reaching any code this pass touched. Fixed the one-line locator (`"Evaluations"` → `"Quality"`).

**i18n**: 1 key (`evaluation.artifactPreviewUnavailable`) added to all 5 locales, phrasing cross-checked against each locale's own existing `evaluation.*` vocabulary.

Verified: `eslint --max-warnings=0`, `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` (chunk budget passed), `npm run architecture:check` (all fitness-test binaries reported 0 failed), `openspec validate --specs --strict` (142/142), all clean (no Rust file touched). `npx vitest run` across `evaluation-center/`, `ui/data-table/`, `ui/evidence/`, `ui/status/`, `src/i18n/`: 15 files / 107 tests, 100% clean. `npx playwright test tests/e2e/evaluation-center.spec.ts` (5/5, live Chromium run, independently re-run after the dispatched sub-agent's own passing run — functional test plus all 4 theme/viewport visual variants, including the 390px-narrow compact-card fallback). All 5 locale files re-verified valid JSON, zero duplicate keys, 5468 keys each.

Detail is a placeholder showing only already-fetched fields (name/agent/frequency/enabled/next-run/status) — occurrence preview and history need 19.6/19.11's own new service calls, not attempted here. First real consumer of `RunsSection.scheduleId`, wired bidirectionally through `onSectionChange` so Back/forward/reload restore the selected task.

- [x] 19.10 Add Run now returning stable operation or Run identity without changing recurrence. — Full stack, both backends (6a47001f): new `run_scheduled_task_now` Tauri command + Web mock + service method + a state-aware button. Reuses `run_one_task` (the sweep's own dispatch function, moved into `contexts/sessions/infrastructure/scheduled_tasks.rs` rather than duplicated) so the on-demand and swept paths can't drift into two different dispatch behaviors. Recurrence is provably untouched: `run_one_task` itself never writes to the database (only its callers' `mark_task_succeeded`/`mark_task_failed` do), and the new path calls neither — `record_manual_run` instead inserts one already-complete `scheduled_task_runs` row, which the sweep's own `completed_at IS NULL` completion query can structurally never pick up. Verified by a dedicated test running both paths interleaved and confirming neither cross-contaminates the other's row. Returns `{run, operationId: null}` matching `MissionControlActionReceipt`'s own shape — a session id wrapped in the existing `ScheduledTaskRun` concept, no new operation concept invented.

Uses this codebase's established `_one`/`_other` plural convention, already enforced by `i18n-resource-parity.test.ts`. Disclosed behavior change: singular is now "Every 1 minute" instead of the old "Every minute", matching every other plural pair already in this codebase.
